# Build history

Every change to Landflow, in order: what each one built and the page that documents it. Part of the
[Landflow docs](README.md). The project was built between 2026-10-25 and 2026-10-29, in 27 commits on `main`, and the
docs were brought up to date on 2026-10-30.
[roadmap.md](roadmap.md) has the detail of each wave: what it built, the bugs found while checking it, what was checked
live and what remains. This page is the map from a commit to those sections.

## How the work was done

- **In waves.** The waves came from a 2026 comparison with other workflow platforms
  ([reports/Workflow platform feature gaps.md](<../reports/Workflow platform feature gaps.md>)) and from the n8n
  research in [research/](research/01-n8n-node-interface.md). Larger features were first written up as plans and their
  open questions settled ([transports.md](transports.md#decisions), [composites.md](composites.md)).
- **Checked three ways.** Each wave ended with every suite passing and a clean type check
  ([development.md](development.md#verification)). It was then checked live: on the local stack, on a scratch host and in
  the browser ([development.md](development.md#checking-a-change-live)). The bugs those checks found were fixed in the same wave and listed
  in its roadmap section.
- **Documented with the change.** Each commit updates its feature page, [reference/](reference/api.md),
  [limitations.md](limitations.md) and [roadmap.md](roadmap.md) ([CLAUDE.md](../CLAUDE.md) states the rule).
- **On this machine only.** Everything ran on the OrbStack Docker engine and its Kubernetes cluster (namespace
  `landflow-dev`). AWS APIs were exercised only against LocalStack, outside services only through local fakes or
  emulators, and with the development credentials in `deploy/.env.example`. No image was pushed and nothing was
  deployed to a cloud ([CLAUDE.md](../CLAUDE.md), "Local only").

## Decisions along the way

| Date | Decision | Recorded in |
|---|---|---|
| 2026-10-25 | Running Landflow agents inside Claude Code and other harnesses is parked; only its spike was run | [harnesses.md](harnesses.md) |
| 2026-10-26 | One messaging core with small broker drivers, chosen per call from live traffic. Every plugin is also an MCP server, and agentgateway is the bundled gateway. Agents sign in through the identity provider or with Landflow MCP tokens | [transports.md](transports.md#decisions) |
| 2026-10-27 | Spotlight is the editor's one AI surface. The Home, ⌘K across the app and the action layer stay a plan until its open decisions are settled | [spotlight.md](spotlight.md), [assistant.md](assistant.md#spotlight) |
| 2026-10-29 | Triage safety rules: fail open, sev1 is never held, the AI can't drop an alert, only people approve noise rules, Shadow before Enforce | [triage.md](triage.md#safety-rules) |
| 2026-10-29 | Composites: the nodes are copied in and the contract is linked. Errors block Publish, warnings don't, and Save is never blocked. Free nodes are allowed unless the type is `closed`, and slots can take nested composites. The UI says "Composite" and "slot" | [composites.md](composites.md) |
| 2026-10-30 | kagent, not Google AX, runs Landflow agents on Kubernetes: A2A in, MCP out, approvals both ways. It runs in a kind cluster, since Agent Substrate needs Kubernetes 1.37 | [a2a.md](a2a.md), [harnesses.md](harnesses.md#other-harnesses) |
| 2026-10-30 | Landflow is the source of a kagent agent's persona: it writes the system prompt its runtime builds, the model and the tools by server-side apply on deploy and on every publish, reports any drift instead of hiding it, and never takes over a kagent agent it didn't write | [a2a.md](a2a.md#landflow-agents-on-kagent) |
| 2026-10-31 | Stage 1 of running Landflow agents in Claude Code goes through kagent's Claude Code Harness rather than a harness of Landflow's own. Plugins come from Landflow's marketplaces, pinned to a commit, and Landflow serves them as OCI images, since kagent's sandbox can't fetch git sources | [a2a.md](a2a.md#on-claude-code), [harnesses.md](harnesses.md#other-harnesses) |
| 2026-11-07 | The roster and Guardrails, settled (`ef80263`): a picked agent starts at the trigger when the flow routes to its target (a Switch or If on `agentKey`), rather than the route running Guardrails itself before starting at the target. The engine already starts at the trigger for the lead, so this adds no engine path, keeps one run, one trace and the author's own refusal branch, and does not guess which guardrail applies; the cost is that an existing flow is only guarded once it is wired this way, which validation warns about | [features/ai.md](features/ai.md#hosted-chat-and-widget) |
| 2026-11-07 | Image tool results touched `packages/engine/src/executor.ts` (a tool's images pass through the executor) and `agents/tools.ts`, outside the workstream's own files; the change is small and covered by `packages/engine/test/tool-images.test.ts` | specs/17-notes/ImageToolResults.md |
| 2026-11-07 | The debug token (W1c) edited files outside its list: `mcp/identity.ts` (an explicit 403 for a debug token at plugin and project MCP endpoints), `mcp/instance.ts` and `instance-route.ts` (the `debug` caller kind and its server), `mcp/instance-tools.ts` (`export` on `executionDigest`), `openapi.ts` (three operations) and `db/schema.ts` (migration 2045 and `TABLES`). A separate table and format rather than an MCP JWT claim, a server of its own rather than a filter, the audit actor anonymous with the minter in the details, and `debugId` rather than `tokenId` (the audit layer drops secret-looking keys) | [specs/19-debug-token.md](specs/19-debug-token.md) |
| 2026-11-07 | The three Vercel prototypes each got a verdict: json-render and the AI SDK plugin stay as flags (opt-in), just-bash is adopted as a flag; versions pinned exact and at least two weeks old. The capped deepsec scan (W5d) was not run: it needs an API key and a cost cap decision | [research/json-render-verdict.md](research/json-render-verdict.md), [research/ai-sdk-verdict.md](research/ai-sdk-verdict.md), [research/just-bash-verdict.md](research/just-bash-verdict.md) |

## 2026-10-31: Run Trace

| Commit | What it built | Documented in |
|---|---|---|
| `42bd0fa` | Run Trace, which shows what a run did, for any flow and phone first:<br>• each execution's journal (`execution_events`, migration 2000) of actors, spans, messages, artifacts and checks. It is written by the runner, Landflow agent sessions, the A2A Agent node (every remote step; `isError` results now fail the step), approvals and the gate. Content is redacted and cut to 32 KB per event (**Content kept**), kept 30 days, and goes out live as `journal` events. `GET /api/executions/:id/events`;<br>• `ctx.artifact` / `ctx.check` in the TypeScript, Python and Go SDKs, `ExecuteResponse.records` in the protocol (proto field 5) and the engine's `node.record`. The GitHub plugin reports pull requests, issues and branches;<br>• the screens: **Run trace** next to Canvas and `/runs/<id>`, with a header, Needs you, the Story, Trace, Team and Report tabs, a detail sheet and a time slider. Needs you and live cards appear on Executions;<br>• five built-in checks, profiles (Generic, Software delivery, Customer support, custom JSON; Workflow settings → Run Trace), the structured reply block, and the **Run Report** node with its gate (send back, ask a person via `POST /api/executions/:id/gate`, or warn). `POST /api/executions/:id/checks`, `GET /api/runs/needs-you`;<br>• older runs get a journal rebuilt from their run data the first time their trace is opened (within the retention). Rebuilding run 2 found that its Reviewer edited three source files, pushed none of it and approved; it also led to fixes in the checks: role rules match an actor's name rather than its description, Claude Code's memory notes don't count as code changes, filtered test runs count, and one check per claim;<br>• the header shows the Run report's verdict, the Report tab can check a run with another profile, and on a phone the app's rail is a drawer behind a top bar;<br>• rounds: after a send-back that was worked on, the report judges the new round's steps only. The GitHub node gets **Pull Request → Merge** (squash, merge or rebase; only if the head is still a given SHA), so a flow can merge once its Run report passes | [run-trace.md](run-trace.md), [protocol.md](protocol.md), [reference/api.md](reference/api.md) |

## 2026-10-31: agent teams and what agents made

| Commit | What it built | Documented in |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `42bd0fa` | Spotlight builds multi-part changes:<br>• a progress-aware budget (up to 20 model calls; two steps in a row that only repeat stop the reply) and a short plan, then one proposal whose new nodes join by `ref`;<br>• proposals not yet applied or dismissed go back with each message (`pending`), show in `get_workflow` as pending and are never proposed again;<br>• an If, Filter or Switch put after a node that already branches (the Run Report's Passed and Send back) goes back to the model once, naming the outputs, and the outline lists each branching node's outputs;<br>• loops: a connection back to an earlier node is allowed, with the round-counter pattern in the prompt. A live test on `qwen3-coder:30b` had added a Filter after the Run report, re-proposed pending nodes and stopped at 8 steps | [assistant.md](assistant.md#the-builder-chat), [spotlight.md](spotlight.md), [reference/api.md](reference/api.md) |
| `42bd0fa` | What a run made, in Run Trace:<br>• an **Artifacts** tab: code changes first (a card per file with +/− counts and one line per agent, **pushed** or **never pushed**, tap for the diff), then Markdown documents, links and JSON data. `lib/shared/run-changes.ts` folds `Write`/`Edit`/`MultiEdit` steps (jsdiff); a change is pushed when the same agent committed that file and pushed. | (this change) | History rows for the team node's last-run fix. |
| (voice) | Voice, step 1: a microphone in Spotlight records, `POST /api/assistant/transcribe` turns it into text through an OpenAI-compatible speech-to-text server (`LANDFLOW_STT_*`), and the text lands in the box to edit. Push-to-talk only; no spoken replies yet. |
| (readme) | The README as a full platform overview: what is in the box, a tour in full-width desktop screenshots (home, plugins, editor, dictation, agents, teams, Run Trace, Marketplace), core concepts, every area, Mermaid architecture diagrams, quick start, local models and dictation setup. |
| (layers: access and checklist) | The last two layers of the plan. **Tool access by caller**: an agent's rules (tool, role / user / source, allow / ask / deny; first match wins) decide which tools a caller sees before a run, through a policy port in `lib/server/agents/policy.ts` whose default adapter is the rules and which can also ask OPA or OpenFGA (`LANDFLOW_TOOL_POLICY`; the most restrictive answer wins, an unreachable engine denies). The response cache looks at the tools before the policy, so a denied caller cannot fill it for an allowed one. **Layers checklist**: the top of Abilities lists the nine layers around the model with a Set up button each, read from the config. |
| (response cache) | Cache, layer B of the layers plan: **Abilities → Response cache** answers a repeated question (case and spacing ignored) from a stored answer with no model call, for the first message of a session to an agent without tools or memory. Keyed on the answer-shaping config, so an edit starts fresh; per-agent TTL, stats and Clear; `agent_response_cache` (migration 2043) behind a `ResponseCache` interface so Valkey or a gateway can replace it. |
| (prompt cache) | Cache, layer A of the layers plan: agents on Anthropic mark the system prompt and the last tool with `cache_control`, so an agent loop pays the cached-read price for the prefix it resends. Always on for agents, no setting; the ledger and Run Trace already priced and showed cached tokens. Checked that kagent has no response or semantic cache (it only reports `cachedInputTokens`), so an exact-match or semantic answer cache stays open. |
| (model router) | The model router of the layers plan: an agent's **Abilities → Model router** sends short, simple requests (at most N characters, no tools unless allowed) to a cheaper model of any provider, shows a notice in the session, and is versioned, diffed and restored with the rest of the config. `lib/shared/agent-router.ts` holds the rule; the runtime picks once per run. No component is added: the cheap model is a plain model setting, so Ollama or an OpenAI-compatible gateway can stand behind it. |
| (eval gate) | From a reel on the eight layers around a model (prompt, RAG, tools, access, guardrails, cache, router, observability), compared with what Landflow has: **version restore** (a version's config back into the draft, with the fields that changed listed in the history) and the **eval gate** (test cases per agent, an LLM-judged run on the draft, and a publish held when the draft scores under 3 / 5 or 0.5 below the published version; migration 2042). Still to build from that list: a model router, a response cache, per-caller tool access, a layer checklist per agent. |
| (layer stats) | The router and cache cards show how many of an agent's recent requests each handled (`GET /api/agents/:id/layer-stats`, read from the newest 200 sessions' notices, so no new table). The Insights page also has an **Agent layers** card (`GET /api/insights/agent-layers`) with the totals across every agent. |
| (semantic cache, test panel, LLM gateway) | The rest of the layers plan. **Semantic cache**: a question with no exact match is embedded (Ollama, `nomic-embed-text`) and answered from the closest stored one at or above a threshold (migration 2044, `agent_semantic_cache`; a `SemanticStore` and an embedder function, so both swap); checked live with the real embedding model (a reworded question: 0.33 s, 92% alike; an unrelated one went to the model). **Tool access test panel**: `POST /api/agents/:id/access/test` and a "What can they do?" panel on the card; `applyToolPolicy` now returns each tool's decision. **LLM gateway**: agentgateway 1.6 in front of Ollama, as a compose profile (`llm-gateway`, :54100) and a Kubernetes example, both run and called for real; kgateway's Envoy-based AI Gateway is deprecated (removal in 2.2) and its docs point to agentgateway, so that is what ships. |
| (review fixes) | A review of the layers branch found, and this fixes: **Restore** merged the version over the draft, so a cache, router or access rules the version never had stayed (it now replaces the config); removing the last **access rule** (or clearing a rule's tool name while typing) did not clear it or dropped the rule; OPA/OpenFGA were asked tool by tool (now all at once); the **eval gate** read and filled the response cache (it now bypasses it) and a **publish** could ship a draft edited while the gate ran (now a 409); an answer the router's cheap model gave was cached for the strong model's questions (no longer); the transcribe route buffered a body with no Content-Length whole (now capped as it is read); the dictation hook could open two recorders. |
| (policy engines) | OPA and OpenFGA as things you can run: the compose profiles `tool-policy-opa` and `tool-policy-openfga` (pinned by digest, loopback), an example Rego policy and OpenFGA model in `deploy/tool-policy/`, `scripts/openfga-tool-policy.sh` (setup, grant, check) and a Kubernetes example; all started and queried for real, in compose and in `landflow-dev` (OPA gave allow, ask and deny as designed; OpenFGA honoured a grant, a missing grant and a wildcard). A Kubernetes ConfigMap mount made OPA load the policy three times, so the example names its two files. |
| (savings) | Insights' **Agent layers** card gets an **Estimated saved** tile, and each router and cache card says "saving about $x": routing is the agent's own model's price for the cheap model's tokens less the cheap model's, the cache is the average cost of the agent's own answers per cache answer (`lib/server/agents/layer-savings.ts`, from the price table; no new storage). |
| (layers in Run Trace) | The last item of the layers plan: in a workflow's run, the agent layers' decisions are checks in Run Trace: *Tool access* (a tool withheld or needing approval for the caller, a warning), *Model router* and *Response cache* (`journal/agent-session.ts`, `layerCheck`). |
| (tsx for the web app) | `apps/web` declares `tsx` (its `worker` script and the `audit-chain` test run it, but only `packages/protocol` had it), so the `audit-chain` test no longer fails on a fresh install. |
| (guardrail gaps) | A second look at the reel's scenes found three gaps in agent guardrails, all built. **Tool-result stage** (`guardrails.toolResult`): what a tool returns is checked before the model reads it, because an email or page the agent reads can carry planted instructions the input stage never saw (a block hands the model a failed-call notice and the run goes on; a sanitize cleans it; shown in the session and as a *Tool result guardrail* check in Run Trace, with the tool's name). **Grounded** output check: the agent's model judges the answer against the passages its knowledge bases returned in the run (`context` option of `runGuardrails`, `BuiltTool.knowledge`); skipped for an agent with no knowledge base. **Stay on topic** (the engine's topical-alignment check) is now in the builder. Tests: `agent-guardrails`, `agent-grounded`. |
| (Jev judge) | TypeSafe AI’s Jev and AWS’s Strands Decider (both speak the System One API, `POST /v1/systemone`; client in `nodes/systemone.ts`, credentials `core.typesafeApi` and `core.strandsDeciderApi`) are a decision model (text and yes/no questions in, a probability out), not a chat model, so it joins as a **guardrail judge** instead of a fourth provider. New `core.typesafeApi` credential and client (`nodes/typesafe.ts`: `POST /v1/systemone`, Bearer key, retries 429/529); `runGuardrails` takes a `judge` that answers the model checks in place of the chat model; an agent rule gets `judge: { provider, credential }`, chosen in the Guardrails card; providers register in `agents/judges.ts` (`registerJudgeProvider`) and the builder lists `JUDGE_PROVIDERS`, so another service is a registration, not a change to the guardrail code. Fails closed. Tests: `typesafe`, `agent-judges`. Open: Decide node, router, cost counting, a live key. |

## 2026-11-12: integrations that report activity

| Commit | What it built | Documented in |
|---|---|---|
| (activity items) | One shape for a change in an outside tool, the **activity item** (`source`, stable `sourceId`, `url`, `actorEmail`, `occurredAt`, a suggested `kind`, `title`, `summary` ≤ 600, team, initiative and customer hints, trimmed `raw`, optional `cursor`): a JSON Schema in `packages/protocol/activity-items`, a builder in each SDK (`activityItem`, `landflow.ActivityItem`, `activity_item`) and shared cases all three SDKs run | [protocol.md](protocol.md#16-activity-items) |
| (Slack activity) | Slack as an activity source: Message → **Get Many Since** (history across channels matching `customer-*`-style patterns since a time or cursor, paged, replies optional, oldest first) and **Get** (one message with full text), User → **Get Many** (members with email), Channel → Get Many by name pattern, the **Slack Activity Trigger** (Events API, signature checked, one activity item per message, lookups cached in static data) and a Channel Name Filter on the Slack Trigger; private channels and DMs only behind an option; tests against a fake workspace | [plugins/messaging](../plugins/messaging/README.md#slack-activity), [integrations.md](features/integrations.md) |
| (Google activity) | Google as an activity source: the **Google Drive Trigger** (Drive's changes feed from a page token, shared drives included, folder path, owner and the first 600 characters of a Doc, customer and initiative hints from `Customers/<name>` and `Projects/<name>` folders), the **Google Calendar Trigger** (sync token, attendees, customer from the first outside domain, held / scheduled / opt-in cancelled, a new baseline on 410), Drive → Get Many Changed Since and Get Item, Calendar → Get Many Changed Since, and the **Google Directory** node (people with email, org unit, title, department and manager, a service account impersonating an admin with the directory scope alone); the fake Google grew a changes feed, shared drives, sync tokens and the Directory | [plugins/google-workspace](../plugins/google-workspace/README.md#activity-items), [integrations.md](features/integrations.md) |
| (Notion activity) | Notion as an activity source: the **Notion Trigger** (polls search by last edit and each root database's data sources, under chosen pages or databases, a 5-minute lookback with the versions already output remembered, author emails from the users list, customer and initiative hints from `Customers/<name>` and `Projects/<name>` ancestors), Page → **Get Many Changed Since** and **Get Content** (nested blocks), User → **Get Many** with email and Comment → **Create**, on `Notion-Version: 2026-03-11` (data sources) while the older operations keep 2022-06-28; 429s wait out `Retry-After`; tests against an in-memory workspace | [plugins/productivity](../plugins/productivity/README.md#notion-activity), [integrations.md](features/integrations.md) |
| (Phase A docs) | What the activity integrations do not do yet (Slack membership and the 2025 history limits, Drive and Calendar polling instead of expiring push channels, Notion search lag and no teamspaces, fakes only) and the roadmap entry with Phases B and C next | [limitations.md](limitations.md#integrations-that-report-activity-built-2026-11-12), [roadmap.md](roadmap.md#integrations-that-report-activity-built-2026-11-12) |
| (operation schemas per resource) | The expression field check (validate_workflow, the debug tools) reads the output schema of the `operation` property shown for the node's resource, not the first one: Notion's page and Slack's message operations (and Twilio's calls) declared schemas the check never found | [limitations.md](limitations.md#debugging-chat-with-agents-browser-and-web-search-built-2026-11-07) |

actions decided by the flow, suggestions with an audience, Find Similar, declared effect metrics, backtesting, a
human-confirm node, the brain over MCP), then the runbooks' follow-ups (a runbook made when a room ends, steps that call
a plugin). The open items of each wave are in its roadmap section and in [limitations.md](limitations.md).

| (this change) | Packaging finish: Browser Continue On Failure for Act on Page; the Browser image built and run on arm64 (Debian chromium on arm64, `HOME=/opt/agent-home`, agent-browser 0.41.2); Agent Shell and Web Search packaged (compose profiles, scan ports 7111-7114/7211-7214, k8s components, `pnpm dev:all:agent-shell`, `pnpm dev:all:web-search`); the Python SDK composite validator checks starter-node retry fields | [deployment.md](deployment.md), [limitations.md](limitations.md) |
| (this change) | Flow detectors: **Detector Trigger** (`core.detectorTrigger`) makes a published workflow the detector `flow:<workflowId>`; the brain runs it after the built-in detectors (tick, Run now, a per-detector **Run** in Insights, `POST /api/brain/run { detector }`) with `quiet`, `minConfidence` and `lastRunAt`; its Suggest Improvement posts as the detector (source `detector`, its own fingerprints, held under the quiet bar), so quieting, 14-day expiry, effect baselines, Insights and Suggestion From work for it; `DetectorId` takes `flow:<id>`; the brain's next nine items in the roadmap | [second-brain.md](second-brain.md#flow-detectors), [roadmap.md](roadmap.md#planned-next) |
| (this change) | Custom event kinds: namespaced kinds declared in `usage_kinds` (migration 2062, `GET/POST /api/usage/kinds`), a **Record Event** node (`core.recordEvent`) that writes them into the ledger, On Usage Event on them with an Attribute Filter, reports grouped and filtered by an attribute (`groupBy=attr`), with tests | [second-brain.md](second-brain.md#6-your-own-conclusions), [reference/api.md](reference/api.md) |
| (this change) | Entities: a registry of initiatives, people, customers and systems (`brain_entities`, migration 2063, `/api/entities`), facts and suggestions about them (`entity:<type>/<id>`), the **Entity** node (`core.brainEntity`), Entity Knowledge, entities in Find Similar, the study, the MCP brain tools and the analyst, with tests | [second-brain.md](second-brain.md#entities), [reference/api.md](reference/api.md) |
| (this change) | Actions decided by the flow: suggestions carry flow-defined buttons (Suggest Improvement's **Actions**), `POST /api/proposals/:id/act`, and **On Suggestion Decided** (`core.suggestionDecidedTrigger`) starts flows on every decision (apply, dismiss, snooze, a button); advice can be applied when a flow listens; a detector filter of `any`, `all` or `*` means every detector, with tests | [second-brain.md](second-brain.md#actions-decided-by-the-flow), [reference/api.md](reference/api.md) |
| (this change) | Suggestions with an audience: addressed to named people (`audience`, the inbox's **For me**), Suggest Improvement's **Send To** and **Mirror to Slack** (DMs with the suggestion's buttons plus Apply, Dismiss and Snooze; a click in Slack or any decision elsewhere keeps both in step); entity suggestions stay visible to the entity's project, with tests | [second-brain.md](second-brain.md), [reference/api.md](reference/api.md) |
| (this change) | Find Similar: `findSimilar` and the **Find Similar** node (`core.findSimilar`): nearest workflows, agents, entities and facts by words, plus vectors when embeddings are on; matches in projects the caller can't open are a six-field projection with no id, no facts and no evidence; Vector Store nodes refuse `landflow-brain*` collections. The vector bonus follows cosine similarity above a floor (0.62), not rank, and the owner falls back to the project's owner, with tests | [second-brain.md](second-brain.md#find-similar), [limitations.md](limitations.md) |
| (this change) | The brain over the instance MCP server (`/api/mcp`): `brain_search_facts`, `brain_list_suggestions`, `brain_workflow_knowledge`, `brain_usage_report`, `check_overlap`, `brain_suggest`, each scoped to what the caller can open, with tests | [second-brain.md](second-brain.md), [reference/api.md](reference/api.md) |
| (this change) | Effect metrics the flow declares: a Detector Trigger (or one Suggest Improvement) names a usage query or a sub-workflow that returns a number, with a label and which way is better; the baseline and the 7 and 14 day checks use it, the outcome respects the direction, the card says **Measured by**, with tests | [second-brain.md](second-brain.md#a-metric-your-flow-declares) |
| (this change) | Backtests: a detector (built-in or flow) reruns as of past points without writing suggestions and is scored against what people decided (precision, recall, misses), with a precision gate, `brain_backtests` (migration 2064), `POST/GET /api/brain/backtest` and a **Backtest** card on Insights, with tests. The backtest flag is an async-local pinned with `singleton()`: a module-level one was a different store in the node registry's module graph under Next.js, so a flow backtest found nothing (seen live, not in vitest) | [second-brain.md](second-brain.md), [reference/api.md](reference/api.md) |
| (this change) | **Ask a Person** (`core.confirmDraft`): sends a draft to a person (execution view, Slack with Approve / Edit / Reject and an edit modal, or email), waits like a tool approval, and continues on Approved or Rejected with the edited text, comment, who and when; a timeout settles with a default, with tests | [features/engine.md](features/engine.md) |

| (this change) | Linear, Attio and Fathom wired in: `pnpm plugin:linear` / `plugin:attio` / `plugin:fathom` and `dev:all`, compose services and scan ports 7116–7118 / 7216–7218, Kubernetes base and OrbStack overlay | [features/integrations.md](features/integrations.md), [deployment.md](deployment.md) |

| (this change) | The Brain page: `/brain` with Ask, work shared across teams, suggestions, teams, pages needing a person, the directory and latest work; a page per entity with a model-written summary (links, numbered references, stale flag; table `brain_pages`, migration 2065), page-scoped Ask, work, people, related pages, facts and mentions; four routes under `/api/brain/wiki` and `/api/brain/ask`; What Landflow knows moved from Insights | [second-brain.md](second-brain.md#the-brain-page), [reference/api.md](reference/api.md) |
| (this change) | The Brain page as an editor's workbench: activity bar (Explorer with Open Editors, Outline and Backlinks; Search; Timeline; What Landflow knows as a tab), the brain as Markdown files (`index.md`, `log.md`, folders per type and department, marks for work, shared and problems), preview tabs, breadcrumbs, Preview and Source (frontmatter, links, references), quick open with commands, Problems and Suggestions panel, status bar, Spotlight at the bottom of the editor about the open file; one catch-all route; people's department on wiki entries | [second-brain.md](second-brain.md#the-brain-page) |
| (this change) | The AI flow builder's wait for the model is `LANDFLOW_BUILDER_TIMEOUT_SECONDS` (default 600, was a fixed 180 s): with every plugin online the node catalog is long, and a dense local model (qwen3.8 27B read it at about 64 tokens/s) timed out before writing; a timeout now says the model was too slow and names the setting instead of "Could not reach Ollama", with tests | [assistant.md](assistant.md), [reference/configuration.md](reference/configuration.md) |
| (this change) | The AI flow builder (Describe a workflow, Spotlight's first draft) runs on `anthropic/<model>` and `openai/<model>` as well as Ollama, through the model adapters: the schema goes in the system prompt, the JSON object is taken out of the reply, 16,000 output tokens and the server-side refusal fallback on Claude; before, `LANDFLOW_BUILDER_MODEL=anthropic/…` reached the chat assistant but sent the first draft to Ollama under that name, with tests against a fake Anthropic API | [assistant.md](assistant.md), [reference/configuration.md](reference/configuration.md) |
| (this change) | Spotlight and the AI flow builder know your agents: the published agents the caller can see go in their prompts beside the teams (`roster()`, `agentBrief()`: what each does, its role, skills and tools), with a rule to run one that fits through Run an Agent, use a plain AI Agent for a small step, and otherwise say a dedicated agent is needed. Spotlight then proposes `newAgent`; applying it adds the Run an Agent node and saves the agent as a draft (`POST /api/agents` now takes `description` and `instructions`), which a person gives tools and publishes. An agent's name where its id goes becomes the id; a made-up id is cleared. Before this, only teams were listed, so Spotlight couldn't pick one of your agents or tell that a new one was needed. |
| (this change) | Spotlight's autonomous mode, on by default: a flow drafted on an empty canvas goes straight into Test and fix, and the changes a chat reply proposes are applied as it ends (one step, one Undo) and the flow is tested and fixed, with no Apply or Test and fix click. Publishing and agents it creates still wait for a person. An Autonomous switch in the conversation's header turns it off (per browser). |
| (this change) | Spotlight's new agents are designed, not bare: `POST /api/agents/design` has the Agent architect pick a model this host has (local Ollama first), the tools it needs and a knowledge base (an existing one, or a new empty one embedded with a local embeddings model), with `list_models` and `list_knowledge_bases` in the architect for everyone. A local model's long `propose_agent` call failed in Ollama's XML tool-call parser (*unexpected EOF*), so the design's first try hands it the models, knowledge bases and matching nodes (`designBrief`) and offers one flat `propose_design` call. In autonomous mode the agent is published so the flow can run it, and a test run waits for it. A new AI Agent step with no local chat model becomes a new agent (`asNewAgents`); a Webhook or Form trigger with no usable test items gets one made up from the fields the steps read (`sampleItems`); tool-call tags a model leaves in its text are dropped. |
| (this change) | Spotlight as one panel at the right of the canvas instead of a bar, a window and a card over the flow (a Fable review set the design): a column on a desktop that folds to a strip, a sheet under the canvas on a phone (the run logs give way to it there); a header that says what it is doing; a timeline of runs, fixes and the agents it designs; a dismissible *Fixed: …* bubble on each node a fix changed (gone after a minute); no toasts for its own runs or autonomous applies; the app's left menu folds to icons. A second Fable review of the result found ten problems, fixed here: Stop now keeps an autonomous reply's half-written proposals from being applied, the editor fits under the phone bar, chat and Spotlight take turns on a phone, a message no longer resets your zoom, New conversation leaves no stray text, the timeline stays where you scrolled, one fix bubble per fix, the header's verdict is about your last request, focus follows fold and unfold, and the panel is folded by default on tablets. Also: "a specialist" or "an expert" now means a new agent, never Ask a Person, and leaked `<parameter>` tags are stripped from replies | [assistant.md](assistant.md#the-spotlight-panel) |
| (this change) | Spotlight's hand-offs and voice, from two Fable reviews (chat and automation; the look, watched live): a **Needs you** row instead of spent fixes when only a person can go on (a credential, an agent to pick, no trigger) and before a test sends for real (**Run it for real**), with the button that does it; the same failure after a fix undoes it and stops; a fix that misses the failing step, re-adds a removed node or adds a No Operation is sent back once; one review per session, publish only on a "Yes"; fix rows titled from their ops (`opsTitle`) with **Undo** on the newest; one Spotlight chip per node (*Fixing…*, *Designing its agent…*, *Needs you*, the last fix) and Spotlight's applies no longer select nodes; replies of at most two short sentences, without narration or leaked markup, and the model told when autonomous mode applies its change (`session: "auto"`); bubbles that follow the outcome, and no chat-model bubble on Run an Agent; header *Needs you: …* / *Stopped at “X”*; a crash when a fix inserted before a node it also removed; Spotlight's runs make no toast even after the session ended (the run command says who asked); an undone fix leaves no bubble | [assistant.md](assistant.md#test-and-fix) |
| (this change) | Spotlight's look, from the fluency plan's wave 2 and a Fable review of it: a session reads as a ledger (*Run 3 · “Post to Slack” · No Bearer Auth credential selected*, with **more** for the rest; runs numbered on through the conversation; a finished session folds to *Tested 2× · 1 fix · passed*, *stopped* when an outcome follows); an outcome card when a session stops (**Open “X”**, **Undo all fixes** while nothing changed since, **Try again**); the camera goes to the node Spotlight fixes or needs you at, panning on a desktop only when it is out of the clear canvas; a draft counts its seconds, Stop (both) cancels it, as do New conversation and another workflow, and its nodes and connections fade in left to right; one set of header colours; on a phone only, an icon-only Autonomous switch, a short placeholder (`usePhone`), no ⌘I hint, the Execute bar out of the way while Spotlight works, and the bubbles fold a moment after the box loses focus so a tap lands. Fixed on the way: a selector that re-rendered forever and took the panel down, every proposal of an apply counted and undone together, the store's `edits` count, and undoing a replaced graph recorded so ⌘Z brings it back | [assistant.md](assistant.md#the-spotlight-panel) |
| (this change) | Landflow on a phone, part 1 of the fluency plan's wave 3: a bottom tab bar (Home, Flows, Runs with the Needs-you count, Agents, More, which opens the grouped rail) in place of the top bar and its drawer; the editor, an agent and a run's trace full screen with their own back arrow; each page's header as an app bar (title whole, the main action beside it, the rest below, CSS only); the editor's header in two rows; 16 px text fields, `viewport-fit=cover` with safe areas, a skip link, toasts above the tab bar. Desktop screenshots of 14 pages match the ones taken before, except for live data | [mobile.md](mobile.md) |
| (this change) | Landflow on a phone, part 2 of wave 3: Executions as cards on a phone (workflow, status, how long, when; no id or mode); no page scrolls sideways at 390 px (plugin chips wrap, the Developers page breaks long code, 16 px page padding); a **Filters** button and bottom sheet (`PhoneFilters`, `Sheet` with dialog role, focus kept inside, Escape, scroll lock) for Marketplace, Templates, Agents and Search runs, whose **Show results** runs the search; the Agents segment wraps in the sheet. Desktop screenshots of 14 pages match the ones taken before, except for live data | [mobile.md](mobile.md#lists-and-filters) |
| (this change) | Landflow on a phone, part 3 of wave 3: tap a step to select it and tap again to open it; no zoom rail, and Undo and Redo in the one canvas toolbar with 44 px buttons; the node editor full screen with a back arrow, Input, Parameters, Output and Settings one at a time, and a foot bar with ‹ **Run step** › (Run step shows the output; the pane stays as you move between steps; Breakpoint and Run from here in Settings); toasts above that bar. A Fable review of it caught a blank editor when stepping back to a trigger, Settings carried into the next open, a single tap opening a step deselected some other way (now: open only a step that was already selected), and a clipped name. The desktop canvas and node editor are unchanged (shot before and after) | [mobile.md](mobile.md#the-canvas-and-the-node-editor) |
| (this change) | Landflow on a phone, part 4 of wave 3: the editor opens on a **Steps** view on a phone, the flow as cards in run order (`stepOrder()`: from the triggers along main connections, branches indented under their output's name, a merge listed after all its branches back at the split's depth, loops as *then “X”*, sub-nodes and theirs as chips on their step), each with its last run (items and time, the error, a spinner); a tap opens the full-screen node editor, **Add a step** goes on from the last step that doesn't go on yet, **Steps \| Canvas** switches; the Logs panel is hidden in the Steps view. A Fable review caught the main line staying indented after a merge (so Add a step wired beside a branch), nested sub-nodes missing, an unnamed lone output, stale branch labels and the Brain card over the list. The desktop editor is pixel-identical | [mobile.md](mobile.md#the-steps-view) |
| (this change) | Landflow on a phone, part 5 of wave 3: Spotlight's sheet has three heights on a phone, its bar (now the default there, so the steps show), peek (a third of the screen, the newest of the timeline) and half; drag its header for the next one, the chevron goes between the bar and half; still only the person moves it. The desktop editor is pixel-identical | [mobile.md](mobile.md#spotlights-dock) |
| (this change) | Landflow on a phone, part 6 of wave 3: Settings as a list of sections with their hints on a phone, one section at a time with **‹ Settings** back (the `#section` still links straight to one); an agent's save state read out as *Saved* on a phone. Desktop Settings unchanged | [mobile.md](mobile.md#settings-and-agents) |
| (this change) | Landflow on a phone, part 7 of wave 3: a **{}** beside each parameter that takes a dropped field opens a sheet of the input's fields (with samples) and a tap inserts `{{ $json.field }}` as a drop does (`fieldList` in `data-view.tsx`); a collection's Name, Type and Value stack on a phone and its remove button shows without hovering. The desktop node editor is unchanged | [mobile.md](mobile.md#the-node-editor) |
| (this change) | Landflow on a phone, part 8 of wave 3, from a Fable audit: menus and confirmations (`Popover`) open as bottom sheets on a phone; the shared dialog shell, credentials and data-table dialogs rise as sheets; the node picker and version history go full screen; `useDialogFocus` keeps focus in the topmost dialog and gives it back (the Sheet, those dialogs, the node editor and the More drawer use it on a phone); 44 px targets across the shell, Spotlight, the node editor, Executions and the canvas menu; the Runs badge, Executions links and table headings read out properly; a spoken run line in the Steps view; Off steps dashed instead of faded. Desktop menus and dialogs unchanged (shot before and after) | [mobile.md](mobile.md#sheets-and-dialogs) |
| (this change) | Landflow on a phone, the leftovers: a marketplace plugin's drawer takes the screen and **Add marketplace** rises as a bottom sheet, both keeping the focus in (44 px Close); the Run Trace's sheets and **Debug access** keep Tab inside on every screen and hand the focus back; the `window.confirm` prompts (transports, secrets providers, `$secrets` in parameters, the MCP token, a suggestion's action) ask in a sheet on a phone through `confirmAction`, and stay the browser's own on a desktop. Desktop shots pixel-identical. | [mobile.md](mobile.md#sheets-and-dialogs) |
| (this change) | UI alignment, wave 1 (from a Fable review of the AI surfaces): Spotlight's timeline stays at its end when its size changes, so Apply and Publish aren't cut off; one Stop while a reply streams; past turns keep how they were applied when Autonomous is flipped; the building steps stand where the change card will; change cards read a Set's fields as `name = value`, not JSON; a change sent back to the model reads *Reworked the change*; the Autonomous switch is filled when on and icon-only while busy; a one-line placeholder; **Why?** opens what a change left out (no hover); 44 px Undo, **Show more** and the node chip × on a phone; no send while an IME composes; a 5-second Undo toast on a phone. | [assistant.md](assistant.md#the-spotlight-panel) |
| (this change) | UI alignment, wave 2: one chat kit (`components/ui/chat.tsx`) for Spotlight, the editor's Chat, an agent's test chat and Sessions, the Agent architect and the brain: the same bubble, reply mark, Markdown replies (bullets and line breaks kept), meta line with Copy, message box with Stop, working line, error with Try again and Details, and empty state with starters; the brain gets Stop, a multi-line box and lists in answers; 44 px chat controls on a phone, the architect's **Chat \| Draft** switch, the brain's suggestions on a card; tool approvals read Approve / Deny in the editor's Chat; guardrail notices use the warn tokens. | [assistant.md](assistant.md#chat-surfaces) |
| (this change) | UI alignment, wave 3: a suggestion's *why* is four lines with **Show more**, its risk capitalised and its confidence *60% sure* (Home, the canvas card, the editor's suggestions); the analyst's buttons and the inbox's **For me** are the shared buttons; **Show more** for cut text everywhere; the decision card reads *Needs you · …* (or *Waiting for* a named approver), **Details** in its header and **Cancel** for an edit; the Story says *waiting for a person*; agent cards use the colour tokens; a table of the words to use in [ui-review.md](ui-review.md#words). | [ui-review.md](ui-review.md#words) |
| (this change) | UI alignment, wave 4a: one pill switch (`Segmented`: a tab list with ← →, 44 px and scrolling sideways on a phone) on Insights, Marketplace, Developers, Plugins, a project, Explore, the workflow tabs, Executions, an execution's preview, Templates, the composite dialog, a run's data view and logs; one search field (`SearchInput`: × and Escape clear it, 44 px on a phone) on Workflows, Agents, Marketplace, Templates, Plugins, Data tables and Variables; one filter chip (`FilterChip`) for Marketplace sources and Templates and Marketplace categories; the same keyboard focus ring everywhere; every checkbox in the accent colour, **Changed only** and **Flagged only** as `Checkbox`, the Executions sidebar's Auto refresh a switch like the page's; the phone's Filters button no longer stretches. Desktop changes too: the switches gain arrow keys and a focus ring, search fields are one height, category chips are 28 px. Notes in [ui-review.md](ui-review.md#controls). |
| (this change) | UI alignment, wave 4b: one page layout. Every detail page goes back the same way (← and the parent's name, then `/` and the title; the arrow alone on a phone): templates, a marketplace flow, a data table, an agent, a project (new), a run and Compare runs. The run page's header is the shared one (24 px in, back to **Executions**, titled *Run trace*) and the sidebar marks **Executions** on it; header buttons are all 28 px, and 44 px on a phone, where the main action stays on the title row even with a hidden file input (Workflows, Templates); `PageBody width="narrow"` (980 px, left-aligned) replaces six different widths, so Runbooks and Search runs no longer centre; Search runs uses desktop-sized fields on a desktop; Executions on a phone has the 16 px gutter and a card per run. Desktop changes: the back links, header button sizes, widths, the run page header and Search runs' fields. Notes in [ui-review.md](ui-review.md#page-layout). |
| (this change) | UI alignment, wave 5a: a run is *Succeeded* or *Failed* everywhere (rows, logs, the run page, Search runs, a node's runs, an agent's sessions) and Running is amber in every dot and badge, Succeeded green in the Insights chart; one dialog (`components/ui/dialog.tsx`) behind the agent builder's, the data tables' and the rest, its subtitle on two lines, Cancel on the credential dialog, no icons on dialog primaries, sheets on the same overlay; **New runbook** (with +) opens that dialog instead of pushing a form into the page; **New credential**, and empty pages' buttons say what the header says, in outline; `EmptyState` takes an icon and the Teams tab uses it, its **New team** moving to the header; one `StatCard` for Home and Insights (the change no longer wraps, *vs previous 7 days* no longer cut); `stamp()` / `dayTime()` for times in version history, source control and the audit log; a project's **New …** sits in the header. Desktop changes: all of these. Notes in [ui-review.md](ui-review.md#dialogs). |
| (this change) | UI alignment, wave 5b: words, type and small strays. Marketplace draws a saved template with the Templates card (`TemplateCard`: *Needs a plugin* top right with its icon) and a plugin's language with `LanguageBadge`; *Built-in* is spelled one way (Marketplace, Templates, the node creator, Spotlight). Header counts always read *N things* through one `plural()` in `lib/client/format.ts` (Home's *1 plugin online · 1 language*, *56 workflows*, *0 runbooks*, templates, agents, runs, tables, projects, plugins), which replaces seven local copies. Headings have three steps (15, 18, 22 px): an execution's date and the Agents and Teams empty states come down from 20 to 18. Half-step font sizes are rounded onto the 11/12/13/15 scale in the composite panel and dialog, the team node panel, a run's artifacts and the Brain workbench, whose status bar is a quiet `bg-sf2` strip instead of the accent fill. *No evaluation trigger*, *Execution ID* and *Run trace* in sentence case; the node panel's, the agent builder's and the capabilities card's tabs carry real labels instead of CSS `capitalize`. The editor says *step* (**Run step**, *Paused before this step*, *Step name*) and the Workflows list *N steps*; *node* stays on plugin surfaces. The rail's user button shows the email under the name, Home drops its **Build a plugin** button (the rail has it), Settings' nav is 240 px with hints that wrap, and on a phone the Agents persona links sit on their own row. Desktop changes: all but the persona row. Notes in [ui-review.md](ui-review.md#type). Also: white text on green, red, amber and violet fills is `text-bg` (readable in dark: the run timeline, building steps, team strip, a node's badges), and the Agents page's empty state is the shared one in a card, like Teams' (**New agent** in outline, **Ask the Agent architect**). |
| (this change) | A constitution and Claude Code agents for this repository: [constitution.md](constitution.md) holds the rules every research and implementation starts from (non-negotiables, architecture, security and observability baselines, UI, tests, docs, a definition of done and the feature workflow), with the backend refactor's conventions marked *target* until their package lands; `CLAUDE.md` imports it so every session reads it. Fourteen subagents in `.claude/agents/` (researcher, planner, implementer, security and code reviewers, integrator, and specialists for data, observability, UI, AI, plugins, tests, docs and local operations; planning on opus, implementation on sonnet, reviews on fable) and a `landflow-feature` skill that runs a feature from research to a PR. No code changes. | [development.md](development.md#working-with-claude-code-constitution-agents-feature-skill) |
| (this change) | Backend hardening P01: a main shuts down gracefully (`lib/server/lifecycle.ts`: phased shutdown on SIGTERM / SIGINT within `LANDFLOW_SHUTDOWN_SECONDS`, new requests get 503 while draining, `NEXT_MANUAL_SIG_HANDLE` set by `pnpm start` and the Docker image), a failed required boot step exits and a later call retries, optional services that fail to start are logged and counted, unhandled rejections and uncaught exceptions are handled, and the worker uses the same registry | [deployment.md](deployment.md) |
| (this change) | Backend hardening P02: typed configuration. `lib/server/config/` has parsers that never throw or give NaN (invalid values fall back with a `config.invalid` warning), `profile()` (`LANDFLOW_PROFILE`, production by default under `NODE_ENV=production`), `publicUrl()` and `ollamaUrl()` read at call time in place of nine and thirteen copies, and a pure `checkConfig()` that flags development secrets, a missing public URL and an open plugin registry in production. The timeouts and intervals that could be NaN (app webhook retry, plugin call timeout, Kubernetes resync, trace sampler ratio, agent channel poll, MCP approval TTL and wait) now fall back to their defaults. The compose and Kubernetes stacks set `LANDFLOW_PROFILE=development`. | [deployment.md](deployment.md#production-profile) |
| (this change) | Backend hardening P03: Postgres mode never drops a write batch. A batch Postgres rejects five times (not a connection failure) blocks the store instead of being discarded: the batch stays queued, `Database.write` throws `StoreUnavailableError` (status 503, not yet mapped by `handler()`), it is retried every `LANDFLOW_PG_BLOCKED_RETRY_SECONDS`, and `LANDFLOW_PG_SKIP_BATCH=<seq>` sets it aside in `postgres-rejected-<seq>.jsonl` at boot. The spool is one descriptor appended with `writeSync` and fsynced by `LANDFLOW_PG_SPOOL_FSYNC`. `Database.writeHealth()` and the `landflow.db.write_*` metrics report the queue. | [postgres-write-queue.md](features/postgres-write-queue.md) |
| (this change) | Backend hardening P04: execution retention prunes in SQL on columns (age, then count, in chunks of 1,000, through `store.deleteExecution`) and runs from the save path only every `LANDFLOW_RETENTION_EVERY` finished runs (default 50) instead of listing and parsing every summary on each run; the hourly timer and Prune now are unchanged in effect. | [settings and data](features/settings-and-data.md) |
| (this change) | Backend hardening P05: every SCIM change is an audit event (`user.provision`, `user.update`, `user.deactivate`, `user.reactivate`, `user.remove`, `group.create`, `group.update`, `group.members_change`, `group.remove`) credited to the token's user and key id with before and after values, and a rejected SCIM token is `scim.auth_failed` (denied, once per address per minute). **Stop all** (`POST /api/executions/stop-all`) needs an owner or admin and its button is hidden from members. Members' AI usage report is limited to the workflows and agents they can open (another project's ids answer 404), and `GET /api/binary/:id` answers 404 for a file of an execution whose workflow the caller can't open. Notes in [audit-and-governance.md](features/audit-and-governance.md), [files.md](features/files.md). |
| (this change) | Backend hardening P06: one runtime for main and worker. `lib/server/runtime.ts` is a service table (`SERVICES`, `startRuntime({ role })`) that `bootstrap.ts` and `cluster/worker-main.ts` both use, so a service can't be added to one and forgotten in the other. The production check (`checkConfig()`) is the first required boot step: in the production profile a development secret or a missing setting stops the boot with a `ConfigError` naming every variable, and every boot logs `effective configuration` (secrets never appear: they show as `[set]` or `[redacted]`). `handler()` and `scimRoute()` wait on `whenReady()` instead of importing `bootstrap.ts`, which closes the bootstrap, mcp, scim cycle: the static import closure of `http.ts` drops from 401 files to 72. | [architecture](architecture.md), [deployment](deployment.md) |
| (this change) | Backend hardening P07: the probes split. `GET /api/health/live` does no I/O (Next holds requests until boot ends, so a Kubernetes startupProbe covers a slow boot); `GET /api/health/ready` checks boot and draining, the database, the Postgres write queue (`LANDFLOW_READY_MAX_WRITE_LAG_SECONDS`), the schema version and Valkey in queue mode, and answers 503 otherwise, with check names and timings only. Kubernetes liveness and readiness, the Docker health check and the smoke script use them; `/api/health` shows plugin and run counts only to callers with a session or API key | [Deployment](deployment.md#probes) |
| (this change) | Backend hardening P08: plugin registration is closed outside development. In the production profile an announce without `LANDFLOW_REGISTRY_SECRET` is refused (401 on the route, UNAUTHENTICATED on gRPC) unless `LANDFLOW_REGISTRY_OPEN=true`; with the secret every announced endpoint must prove it holds it, as a discovered one does; without it an endpoint on a new host cannot join an existing plugin (409, `LANDFLOW_REGISTRY_ALLOW_JOIN=true` allows it); joins and version changes are audit events (`plugin.endpoint_added`, `plugin.updated`); the announce route returns a generic 422 and logs the detail; metadata and link-local endpoints are refused in production | [plugins](plugins.md#registration-rules) |
| (this change) | Backend hardening P09: client addresses are read through `LANDFLOW_TRUSTED_PROXIES` (default 0: the last `X-Forwarded-For` entry; N proxies: the entry N from the end), forwarded host and proto count only behind trusted proxies, and invite and reset emails refuse to use a request header for their link in production (set `LANDFLOW_PUBLIC_URL`). Resume URLs end in `?sig=` (an HMAC of the execution id) where `LANDFLOW_RESUME_SIGNATURES` is `required` (the production default); waits saved before keep resuming without it. | [authentication.md](features/authentication.md#client-addresses-and-emailed-links) |
| (this change) | Backend hardening P10: the proxy sets `X-Content-Type-Options`, `Referrer-Policy`, `Permissions-Policy`, `X-Frame-Options: DENY` and (over https) HSTS on every response, and pages get a per-request nonce Content Security Policy (`LANDFLOW_CSP` enforce / report / off; enforce in production, report-only in development; `LANDFLOW_HSTS=off` opts out). Chat, forms, stored files, MCP and webhook paths keep their own policy and stay embeddable. `x-powered-by` is no longer sent. Checked live under `LANDFLOW_CSP=enforce` on a production build (desktop and 390 px, with a violation listener): the only violation was zod's `new Function` probe in the browser, now skipped in `packages/protocol/src/schema.ts`. The review then found the Run Trace's PDF preview (an `<object>`) blocked by `object-src 'none'`; the policy now allows `'self' data: https:` there and in `frame-src`, which Chrome also checks for a PDF `<object>`. | [deployment.md](deployment.md#security-headers-and-content-security-policy) |
| (this change) | Wave 1 carryover: retention. `LANDFLOW_RETENTION_EVERY=0` (or a fraction) now counts as 1 instead of silently becoming 50, a prune pass stops if a full chunk deleted nothing, and Settings → General counts stored and prunable executions with two SQL counts instead of reading every summary. The cap's overshoot between passes is in limitations. | [settings and data](features/settings-and-data.md) |
| (this change) | Wave 1 carryover: the Postgres write queue. `handler()` answers `StoreUnavailableError` (a blocked store) with 503 and `Retry-After: 30` instead of 500; a blocked sink closed while it waits no longer retries once on the ended pool; `LANDFLOW_PG_BLOCKED_RETRY_SECONDS` has a floor of one second and the three `LANDFLOW_PG_*` variables are read through `lib/server/config`; a boot that fails after the sink is made closes its spool descriptor and metrics source; the two write counters are created once per meter provider. The recovery section says how a synchronous throw from a background writer can become a restart loop. | [Postgres write queue](features/postgres-write-queue.md) |
| (this change) | Wave 1 carryover: stopping the host. Shutdown steps are registered as soon as the database is open (a signal during the rest of the boot still flushes and releases the lease); requests in flight wait at most 60% of the soft budget; the local-runs poll ends at the deadline and never holds the process; a failed required boot exits only under `NODE_ENV=production`, and `instrumentation.ts` catches the failed boot elsewhere, so `pnpm dev` keeps running (routes answer 500, readiness 503) and retries on the next request; the uncaught-exception handler is exported and tested; `LANDFLOW_SHUTDOWN_SECONDS` is read through `lib/server/config`. The compose mains get `stop_grace_period: 30s` and the workers 45 s, so Docker no longer kills them mid-flush. Stopping the host says which of these follow `NODE_ENV` rather than the profile. | [deployment](deployment.md#stopping-the-host) |
| (this change) | Wave 1 carryover: the queue stack's load balancer (`deploy/queue/lb.mjs`) asks each main's `/api/health/ready` every 2 s and skips one that is not ready, so a main draining for shutdown stops getting requests before its port closes instead of answering 503s. | [deployment](deployment.md#queue-mode-two-mains-two-workers) |
| (this change) | Wave 1 carryover: access scope and SCIM audit. A member's AI usage report grouped per workflow (or agent) lists only the ones they can open, even for a row that also names an agent (or workflow) they can; `executionId` of a run still going is found through the runner, as the file route does; Stop all keeps the `executions:write` key scope (the CLI's `executions stop --all` works again for an admin's key) and still needs an owner or admin; `scim.auth_failed` is capped at 60 a minute in all and a 403 names the key and its user; tests cover the role change a mapped SCIM group causes. | [audit and governance](features/audit-and-governance.md) |
| (this change) | Wave 1 carryover: configuration. The effective-configuration log redacts credentials in each entry of a comma-separated URL list (`LANDFLOW_PLUGINS`); the config parsers and `checkConfig` share one secret-name pattern; configuration.md says which readers take the flag spellings and gives the two new minimums. | [configuration](reference/configuration.md) |
| (this change) | Wave 1 carryover: the generated `encryption.key` is written to a temporary file and hard-linked into place, so processes (or test files) that start together on one data directory agree on one key and never read a half-written file; this fixes the intermittent 'Invalid key length' in the host suite. | [secrets and environments](features/secrets-and-environments.md) |
| (this change) | Wave 1 carryover: the roadmap's Backend hardening section lists what wave 2 applied from the wave 1 reviews and the five items deferred to later packages, each with why. | [roadmap](roadmap.md#backend-hardening-2026-11) |
| (this change) | The constitution, the Claude Code agents and the `landflow-feature` skill describe the code waves 1 and 2 built: the target marks of P01 to P10 are gone and name what exists (`lib/server/config` readers, the `SERVICES` table in `runtime.ts`, `onShutdown()`, `pruneExecutions` / `retentionTick`, `clientAddress()` and `emailedOrigin()`, signed resume URLs, the proxy's headers and CSP, closed plugin registration, `readiness()`). The specialists read the implementer's workflow themselves, the scans catch `.data/` paths, the encryption key and `.org` / `.net` / `.dev` addresses, the integrator and implementer run the file-name scan too, the security reviewer's open findings name their closing package, the skill uses one absolute worktree path, and the gate notes that `pnpm test` reruns the host suite. | [constitution](constitution.md) |
| (this change) | Wave 2 integration: `next build` passes again. Its page-generation workers call `register()` with `NODE_ENV=production`, so the boot's production-profile check refused the build environment and `failBoot` ended the worker; build workers now skip the boot, and the check only warns while `NEXT_PHASE=phase-production-build`. | [deployment.md](deployment.md#production-profile) |
| (this change) | Wave 2 integration: the queue image's worker starts again. The shared runtime brings `next/server` into the esbuild worker bundle, and its compiled ua-parser reads `__dirname`, which an ES module lacks; the bundle's banner now defines `__filename` and `__dirname` next to `require`. Checked with `compose.queue.yml` and `queue-e2e.py` (load, worker takeover, leader takeover all pass). | [deployment.md](deployment.md#queue-mode-two-mains-two-workers) |
| (this change) | P11 (wave 3): `HttpError` (`lib/server/errors.ts`) and a generic 500. `handler()` answers an `HttpError` as `{ error, code, …details }`, a ZodError as 400 and any other error as `Internal error` with the request id (the message goes to the log, not the client); `projectRoute`, `dataTableRoute`, the marketplace wrapper (its regex on message text is replaced by `MarketplaceError` / `FetchError` as `HttpError`s) and `scimRoute` are thin layers over it, the approval email route runs through it, and seven raw `request.json()` calls use `parseBody`. `PUT /api/workflows/:id` refuses a save over a newer version when the client sends `If-Match` or `baseUpdatedAt` (412 `workflow_conflict`; the editor sends it), deleting a workflow is one transaction, and `test/policy-coverage.test.ts` fails a mutating route that has no access rule. Two review fixes: the 412 retry compares the stored workflow with the stored form of the last load or save (`history.stored`, set by `load()` and `markSaved(updatedAt, workflow)`), so a tab that already saved still retries after a publish bump; and the check markers no longer include `currentPrincipal(`, `currentActorId(` or `projectRole(`. | [api.md](reference/api.md), [development.md](development.md#conventions), [editor](features/editor.md) |
| (this change) | Audit coverage (P12): project creation, changes, members and deletion, workflow and credential shares and transfers, instance and project variables, external secrets providers and password changes are audit events with before and after values; failed SAML and OIDC sign-ins, refused reads (once a minute per caller and route), Git sync and data table exports, credential tests and agent tool approvals are recorded too. Per-environment variable values (instance, project and Git sync apply) are `variable.update` events by environment name; SSO failures are limited to one a minute per address; a refused read is recorded even on paths the change log skips. | [Audit](features/audit-and-governance.md) |
| (this change) | P13: outbound calls go through `safeFetch`. In the production profile the HTTP Request node, alert channels, HTTP SIEM destinations, remote MCP servers, RAG embeddings, OpenAI-compatible model calls and OIDC/SAML metadata refuse loopback, private, link-local, CGNAT and metadata addresses (literal IPs, DNS names and every redirect hop) unless `LANDFLOW_OUTBOUND_ALLOW` lists them; development is unchanged. The SDK's `ctx.http` takes an injected fetch and still records its client span. | [governance](features/audit-and-governance.md#outbound-requests) |
| (this change) | Expressions run in a sealed realm (a `node:vm` context with code generation off and no host object reachable; only JSON text crosses), and the production profile runs Code nodes in the code runner unless `LANDFLOW_CODE_IN_PROCESS=true` is set. `async`, `await` and `import()` are refused in expressions. | [engine](features/engine.md#expressions-run-in-a-sealed-realm) |
| (this change) | Database conventions: the prepared-statement cache is a bounded LRU (`LANDFLOW_DB_STATEMENT_CACHE`, default 500), a test enforces that new migrations are numbered above the highest, and list paths in `projects/access.ts` check access with one query per kind instead of one per item. | [development.md](development.md#conventions) |
| (this change) | Wave 2 carryover: `pnpm worker` with an unreachable Valkey exits 1 with `boot failed` after the 5 s ping instead of exiting 0 with no log line (the boot now holds the event loop), and installs its signal handlers before it boots, so a SIGTERM during the boot runs the shutdown steps. The registry is a required step on a worker. A Next host started with `LANDFLOW_ROLE=worker` logs that it runs as a main and its instance id gets the `main-` prefix. | [deployment.md](deployment.md#production-profile) |
| (this change) | Wave 2 carryover: the `effective configuration` log line masks email variables such as `LANDFLOW_OWNER_EMAIL` (`o***@example.com`). | [deployment.md](deployment.md#production-profile) |
| (this change) | Development docs: route tests that forget the `bootstrap` singleton must call `ensureBootstrapped()` themselves (a ready boot no longer reaches it), and live CSP checks use a separate headed Chrome, since the Claude in Chrome extension bypasses CSP in its tabs. | [development.md](development.md#verification) |
| (this change) | Wave 2 carryover: the readiness probe's failing-check set and the trusted-proxies warning flag live in `singleton()`, `queue-e2e.py` waits for a restarted main on `/api/health/ready` instead of `/api/health`, and tests cover `/api/health` for an unknown or expired API key and for a caller lookup that throws. | [development.md](development.md#verification) |
| (this change) | Wave 2 carryover, plugin registration: the metadata-address refusal normalises `grpc://` hosts written as a number or in hex, `guardJoin` is no longer kept on an endpoint's state (it is a per-announce decision), the `plugin registration is open` line is logged by the main's boot even with `LANDFLOW_REGISTRY_GRPC_PORT=off`, and an offline registry-level test covers the 409 join refusal and a brokered-first plugin. | [plugins.md](plugins.md#registration-rules) |
| (this change) | Wave 2 carryover: in the production profile without `LANDFLOW_PUBLIC_URL`, Forgot password, invites and admin reset links answer 503 before any lookup or token (Forgot password answered 500 for an existing account and 200 for an unknown one). The authentication page says to set `LANDFLOW_PUBLIC_URL` when a development host is reached by another name than `localhost`. | [authentication](features/authentication.md#client-addresses-and-emailed-links) |
| (this change) | Wave 2 carryover, docs: the webhook IP allow-list comments point to `clientAddress()` and `LANDFLOW_TRUSTED_PROXIES`; deployment.md says what `LANDFLOW_TRUSTED_PROXIES=0` really changes (behind one appending proxy the address is the same as with 1; the origin is not built from forwarded headers, though HSTS, cookies, the OAuth2 callback, chat and the same-origin check still read them), notes that plain `http:` images are blocked under an enforced CSP, and its proxy and CSP sections move next to the production profile. | [deployment.md](deployment.md#behind-a-reverse-proxy-or-ingress) |
| (this change) | Wave 2 carryover: a signed-in caller skips the resume signature on `/form/waiting/<id>` only for a workflow it may read (the page) or run (the submit); anyone else needs the signed link. | [engine](features/engine.md) |
| (this change) | Wave 2 carryover: `lib/shared/ui-spec.ts` sets zod's `jitless` in the browser before it builds its schemas, so the Run Trace cards' chunk never trips zod's `new Function` probe under the page CSP whichever chunk loads first. | [deployment.md](deployment.md#security-headers-and-content-security-policy) |
| (this change) | Wave 2 carryover: the proxy drops a client-sent `Content-Security-Policy` request header on pages (Next would read the nonce from it in report mode), and a test fails any `<script>` in a page or component without `nonce=`. | [deployment.md](deployment.md#security-headers-and-content-security-policy) |
| (this change) | Wave 2 carryover: the app has a favicon (`apps/web/src/app/favicon.ico`, the sidebar's LF tile), so `/favicon.ico` no longer answers 404 on every page. | [development.md](development.md) |
| (this change) | Wave 2 carryover: `marketplace.test.ts` was flaky because it shared the suite's data directory with `kagent.test.ts`, whose unknown-plugin case fetched the real Anthropic catalog over the network (an unread marketplace is read before a plugin is resolved). The marketplace test has its own data directory and the kagent case stays offline. | [development.md](development.md#verification) |
| (this change) | The constitution, the Claude Code agents and the `landflow-feature` skill describe the code wave 3 built: the target marks of P11 to P15 are gone and name what exists (`HttpError` and the generic 500, the policy fallback test, `api.denied` and the audited permission and secret changes, `safeFetch` and `LANDFLOW_OUTBOUND_ALLOW`, the sealed expression realm and `LANDFLOW_CODE_IN_PROCESS`, the statement-cache LRU, the migration order test and batched list access). The verification rules add that a change to the boot, instrumentation, the proxy or anything the worker imports passes `next build` and the queue stack with `queue-e2e.py` before it merges, and that a test file writing shared state uses its own data directory. | [constitution.md](constitution.md) |
| (this change) | Roadmap: the wave 2 carryover applied in wave 3, and what was deferred with the reason (Postgres ping in readiness, DNS names for plugin endpoints, endpoint audit noise, join toasts, forwarded-header readers, live checks that need a person or real SDKs). | [roadmap.md](roadmap.md#backend-hardening-2026-11) |
| (this change) | Tests: `lifecycle.test.ts` stops the scheduler an earlier boot left running before it points the data directory at a broken database file, so a poller tick under load no longer throws an unhandled error into the suite. | [development.md](development.md#verification) |
| (this change) | `auth/pages.ts` keeps booting through `bootstrap.ts`: `next build` prerenders `/_not-found` in workers where instrumentation registered no bootstrapper, so `whenReady()` there refused the build (found by the wave 3 carryover's `next build` check). | [development.md](development.md#verification) |
| (this change) | Wave 3 review blockers. The editor no longer takes the stored workflow's version after a publish or unpublish: that hid an edit someone else saved before the publish, and the 412 retry already takes the new version when the stored content is what the tab saved. The expression realm also removes `AsyncDisposableStack` (Node 24+, whose `disposeAsync()` makes a promise that can reject and end the host). A test walks the realm's globals for any promise-making name, run on Node 22 and 26. The OpenAI-compatible guard test now fails without the guard (no request may reach the server). | [editor](features/editor.md), [engine](features/engine.md), [limitations](limitations.md) |
| (this change) | Wave 3 integration follow-ups. `safeFetch` always allows the GitHub MCP server (`LANDFLOW_GITHUB_MCP_URL` or its in-cluster default) and `LANDFLOW_OIDC_DISCOVERY_URL`. The production config check warns when Code nodes have no runner. The production defaults for outbound blocking and Code nodes are in deployment.md, and the production CSP recipe says how to reach local fakes. The constitution scopes the "no host objects" rule to expressions, and CLAUDE.md points at `HttpError` and `safeFetch`. | [deployment](deployment.md), [configuration](reference/configuration.md), [development](development.md), [constitution](constitution.md) |
| (this change) | Bounded requests (backend hardening P16). `GET /api/executions` pages by keyset cursor (`limit` 1-500, `nextCursor`) with the member filter in SQL; `limit=Infinity` or `abc` is 400, and five other limit-taking routes validate their query through `parseQuery`. `parseBody` refuses bodies over `LANDFLOW_MAX_BODY_BYTES` (16 MiB) with 413 while streaming. The production profile rate-limits the API per principal (`LANDFLOW_RATE_LIMIT_API`, 600 a minute) and public triggers per address and group (`LANDFLOW_RATE_LIMIT_PUBLIC`, 120), shared through Valkey in queue mode; counters `landflow.http.denied` and `landflow.ratelimit.refused`. The CLI's `runs --status` asks for 500, not 1000. | [api.md](reference/api.md#paging-and-limits) |
| (this change) | P17, logs and spans you can join, wider redaction. Log lines in an API request carry the request id, route, user and API key id; the span gets them too and `/api` responses return `traceparent`. Redaction adds key names (`privateKey`, `passphrase`, `signature`, `connectionString`, DSNs, session tokens, `credentials`), secret-shaped values in every logged string and credential values handed to a node. | [observability.md](observability.md#3-logs) |
| (this change) | Configuration catalog (backend hardening P18). `lib/server/config/catalog.ts` lists every environment variable with its default, description, group, secret flag, production requirement and aliases; `checkConfig` uses it for redaction and the production checks, and `docs/reference/configuration.md` is generated from it (`pnpm --filter @landflow/web gen:config-docs`) in one table per group, plugin-side variables apart. The OTEL per-signal, protocol, sampler and log variables and `LANDFLOW_VALKEY_URL=memory` are documented; the retention variables are catalogued as `LANDFLOW_AI_USAGE_RETENTION_DAYS` and `LANDFLOW_USAGE_RETENTION_DAYS` with the old names as aliases. The SAML, SMTP, S3, marketplace-local and environment-protection flags parse through `envFlag`. Accepted values changed: `LANDFLOW_S3_FORCE_PATH_STYLE=0`/`no`/`off` now turns path style off (it was on for anything but `false`); `LANDFLOW_SMTP_SECURE=1`/`yes`/`on` is now true (was false); `LANDFLOW_MARKETPLACE_ALLOW_LOCAL=true`/`yes`/`on` now enables host-directory marketplaces (was only `1`); `LANDFLOW_ENVIRONMENT_PROTECTED=on`/`off` now decides (was ignored); an unparseable SAML flag now takes its default instead of false (`LANDFLOW_SAML_JIT=<typo>` now provisions users, the default being true). `LANDFLOW_CODE_IN_PROCESS=false` no longer waives the production warning about a missing `LANDFLOW_CODE_RUNNER_URL` (only a true value does). The claim store's `LANDFLOW_CLAIM_ACCESS_KEY_ID` / `LANDFLOW_CLAIM_SECRET_ACCESS_KEY` are catalogued (the secret is refused as a development value in production). `config-catalog.test.ts` fails on an uncatalogued name, a stale page, or more than 88 files reading `process.env` outside `lib/server/config` | [configuration.md](reference/configuration.md), [development.md](development.md#conventions) |
| (this change) | One SSE helper (P19). `sseResponse` bounds what a slow client may leave unread (1 MiB), writes ids, heartbeats and cleans up; the bus keeps the last 500 events; `/api/events` resumes from `Last-Event-ID` (or sends `reset`) and re-checks the viewer's access every 30 s, so a revoked member stops receiving a project's events and a removed session ends the stream. The chat, architect, run and approvals streams use the helper. | [api](reference/api.md) |
| (this change) | The store no longer calls up into features: `saveExecution` emits an execution-finished event and `execution-reactions.ts` records insights, usage and retention (the runtime registers it for both roles), which removes the store, settings, insights and usage ledger import cycle. Every process-wide holder (ten `globalThis` ones, the marketplace sync guard, usage and run caches) goes through `singleton()` with `resetSingleton` and shared `singletonKeys`; `LANDFLOW_USAGE_RETENTION_DAYS` and `LANDFLOW_AI_USAGE_RETENTION_DAYS` replace the old names, which still work. | [architecture.md](architecture.md#storage) |
| (this change) | Wave 4 integration. The configuration catalog lists P16's `LANDFLOW_MAX_BODY_BYTES`, `LANDFLOW_RATE_LIMIT_API` and `LANDFLOW_RATE_LIMIT_PUBLIC` (the generated page had dropped the rows P16 wrote by hand). The static import closure of `http.ts` is re-measured after merging P16-P20: 83 files (the rate limiter and its counters, the catalog through `config/check.ts` in; `insights.ts` out), and its budget follows (76 × 1.1). | [configuration](reference/configuration.md), [development](development.md#verification) |
| (this change) | Workflow delete: `deletePublications` returns its notification and the delete route sends it after the commit, and `forgetWorkflowPolicy` no longer swallows its delete error, so a rolled-back delete announces nothing and a failed policy delete rolls the whole delete back; the atomicity test throws in the last helper too. | [reference/api.md](reference/api.md) |
| (this change) | The SCIM generic 500's `detail` names the request id; `If-Match` takes one entity tag and the millisecond `updatedAt` lets two saves in the same millisecond through (comment and api.md). | [authentication](features/authentication.md), [reference/api.md](reference/api.md) |
| (this change) | Marketplace `detail()` and the flow share page: a plugin read error is a 422 only when the plugin is at fault (a missing local file, without the host path; a bad flow file); a TypeError or other filesystem error is the generic 500. | [limitations.md](limitations.md) |
| (this change) | Audit: a variable PATCH that changes nothing records no `variable.update`; `resource.transfer`'s label names every kind; the folder rename test asserts its real status, which showed the first request booting the host inside its own audit context, so `handler()` now waits for the boot before entering it; the events table says a request with no credential leaves no `api.denied`. | [audit and governance](features/audit-and-governance.md), [development](development.md#verification) |
| (this change) | The password reset route tells `setPassword` it is a reset, so a reset link opened where someone is signed in records only `auth.password_reset`. | [audit and governance](features/audit-and-governance.md) |
