Behind `max-md:` only: the desktop row is unchanged (checked against shots from before, 1440×900) | [mobile](mobile.md) |
| (this change) | Wave 5 review notes, GitHub into the brain (B31): the template keeps a repository under `repoEntityId`'s id (`repo-…`, the same characters), so one repository gets one system page whichever path made it; `POST /api/brain/work` no longer declares the five GitHub-only kinds itself (it gave them the shared fields instead of their own); the template's setup and the integrations page say the same about **Declare Kind If New** (a kind declared from its first event keeps only that event's fields); the briefing page says the template's rows have no owner, so merged PRs in a briefing come from people's own tools | [integrations](features/integrations.md#github-into-the-brain) |
| (this change) | Wave 5 review notes, docs: the velocity section and limitations say commit-status CI flakes aren't paired (only workflow-run attempts are); Marketplace's dead Templates anchor now links the editor page (the briefing nodes' dead `#privacy` anchor went with the privacy fix); the roadmap gets a B31 line and B32 moves below B18's follow-ups; limitations list the GitHub webhook's public URL and the twice-recorded pinned redelivery as bullets; the README's feature map names the nightly velocity flow and GitHub into the brain; the B30 row gets its missing full stop | [roadmap](roadmap.md) |
| (this change) | Test: the Workflows list's phone badge test follows the badge to the row's second line (`LastRunPhone`), checks the desktop badge stays out of a phone row, and that the icon strip gives way on a phone | [mobile](mobile.md) |
| (this change) | Deliver Briefing looks up a Slack member through `safeFetch`, like every other outbound call, so production refuses a credential URL on a private or metadata address | [work and briefings](work-and-briefings.md#the-nightly-sync) |
| (this change) | Wave 5 privacy fix, round 2: every workflow version records who saved it (`workflow_versions.author`, migration 2091), the person whose save made it, read from its own column. A save names its actor explicitly or takes the request's: `createdBy` and `updatedBy` never come from the saved body any more, a save with no person behind it that changes the graph or its pin data clears `updatedBy` and makes a version with no author (it no longer inherits the last saver), and a change that makes no version (a move, a transfer, a publish, activating) credits nobody new. A restore is credited to whoever saved the restored version, not to the person restoring it. Applying a suggestion saves as the person who applies it (a Slack click has no request to take it from), and the publication the `active` flag makes names the saver. Brain test helpers save as the person they name | [editor](features/editor.md) |
| (this change) | The engine ignores pin data in `trigger`, `poll`, `webhook` and `tool` runs and runs the node (`PINLESS_MODES`): pins are saved with the draft but are not part of a version's hash, so a pin could change what a production run does (List People pinned to someone else's id) without a new version; manual, chat and MCP runs still use them. An engine test checks every mode | [engine](features/engine.md) |
| (this change) | Who a run is for, outside the request: an agent's workflow tool runs for the agent's caller (`ToolCaller`): as a sub-execution of the workflow run the agent works in, or for the person in its test chat (the chat route names them), never for whoever's request is around the call, and the runner takes no starter from the request for a tool run. On Suggestion Decided starts its workflows outside the deciding person's request (`outsideRequest()` in `auth/context.ts`). A metric's sub-workflow run is marked `metricOf` (the suggestion it measures), and a retry of a re-run keeps the source's `rerunOf` | [authentication](features/authentication.md) |
| (this change) | Wave 5 privacy fix, round 2 (a security review's two blocking findings): the briefing nodes trust the version that ran, not the draft's last saver. A run nobody started may handle briefings only when an owner or admin saved the version it ran (its recorded author) and, when that is the published version, an owner or admin published it; so a member can no longer publish a version of their own (or an older one) after an owner saved, nor get one in through a save with no person behind it (a Slack click applying a suggestion). The guard also follows what a run came from: the run an automatic retry retries (a made-up or untrusted source is refused), the failed run an error workflow answers, the run a sub-workflow or an agent's tool call came from (the live record first); a metric's measurement run is refused. The refusals say an owner or admin must save a change (and publish it) | [work and briefings](work-and-briefings.md#the-nightly-sync) |
| (this change) | Wave 5 privacy fix, round 3: a member editor could point the Slack credential of the nightly sync at their own server (no version changes) and receive everyone's email address and briefing. Credentials now record who created them and who last changed their data (`createdBy`, `updatedBy`, migration 2092; the same fields in `credentials.json`), from the explicit actor of the form save, Connect (carried in the signed OAuth state), Get token, Disconnect and a Git or environment pull; a change with nobody behind it records nobody, and a token refresh or an encryption key rotation keeps the changer. Deliver Briefing uses its Slack credential only when an active owner or admin last changed it, else skips Slack for everyone with a warning and still sends email. Write Briefing's model and the SMTP settings come from the environment only | [work and briefings](work-and-briefings.md#the-nightly-sync) |
| (this change) | The briefing nodes refuse a run that uses pin data (manual, chat, MCP, a re-run) when the node or any node before it is pinned, in the graph that ran or in the saved workflow: a member's pin-only save on List People (no new version) made an admin's manual run write a briefing for the person and day the member chose. Schedules ignore pins and run as usual. The engine exports `pinsApply(mode)` | [work and briefings](work-and-briefings.md#the-nightly-sync) |
| (this change) | An agent session a workflow run started falls back to that run as its tools' caller only while the run is running or waiting: a session resumed after the run ended (an approval decided later) runs its workflow tools for nobody, not as the ended run's starter or version | [authentication](features/authentication.md) |
| (this change) | Docs: the nightly sync's privacy rules say that four-eyes approval publishes as the member who asked (an owner or admin publishes it themselves), that any owner's or admin's save that changes a version (Insights settings, applying a suggestion, a Git pull) makes them its author, so the publisher is the second key, and that versions imported from the old JSON files have no author | [work and briefings](work-and-briefings.md#the-nightly-sync) |
| (this change) | Briefing nodes: a schedule handles briefings only from the published version: an unpublished draft has only one key (its author), and an owner's or admin's unrelated save can make a member's draft theirs, so a run with no person behind it is refused with "A schedule handles people's briefings only from the published version" unless it ran the published version, saved and published by an owner or admin. The template's setup and the docs say so | [work and briefings](work-and-briefings.md) |
| (this change) | The briefing nodes' refusal for a run with no person behind it now names the published workflow, matching the rule that a schedule handles briefings only from the published version | [work and briefings](work-and-briefings.md) |
| (this change) | Deliver Briefing calls Slack only at `https://slack.com/api`, or at the base URL the instance sets in `LANDFLOW_BRIEFING_SLACK_URL` (server-side, for a proxy or a test double): a Slack credential whose Base URL is anything else is skipped with a warning, even when an owner set it. The skip warning now says a member or an automatic process changed the credential last, and that an owner or admin must check its Base URL and token and save a change | [work and briefings](work-and-briefings.md) |
| (this change) | Credentials: only a save that changes a value makes its caller the last changer (`updatedBy`): an empty `data`, the form saved as it was or a rename vouch for nothing. A token operation (Connect, Get token, Refresh now, Disconnect) never raises trust: an owner's or admin's keeps the last changer, anyone else's records them. A field a credential type marks `hidden` with a default (Slack OAuth2's grant and token URLs) is never stored, so the API can't change it. So after a member repoints the briefing's Slack credential, an owner's Disconnect, Get token, Connect or unchanged save no longer makes it trusted | [work and briefings](work-and-briefings.md) |
| (this change) | The credential dialog sends only the fields that were edited (none: no `data`), so saving it unchanged or renaming it vouches for nothing, and shows "Last changed by <name>" (`updatedByName` on `GET`/`PATCH /api/credentials/:id`) so an owner or admin sees when a member changed it last | [work and briefings](work-and-briefings.md) |
| (this change) | Deliver Briefing skips Slack, with a warning, when its trusted Slack credential has no token (never connected or disconnected), instead of failing the run for everyone; email delivery goes on | [work and briefings](work-and-briefings.md) |
| (this change) | Wave 5 privacy fix, round 6 (closes the class): Deliver Briefing uses no credential from the vault. Five reviews kept finding ways for a member who can edit a project's Slack credential to redirect the nightly delivery of other people's briefings (the last: her own workspace's token, then an owner's edit of another field made the owner the last changer). Like email (`LANDFLOW_SMTP_*`), Slack delivery now takes only server configuration: the bot token in the new secret `LANDFLOW_BRIEFING_SLACK_TOKEN`, sent to `https://slack.com/api` or `LANDFLOW_BRIEFING_SLACK_URL`. With no token, each person who chose Slack gets "Slack delivery skipped: Slack is not set up on this server. An admin sets LANDFLOW_BRIEFING_SLACK_TOKEN …" and email goes on. The node's credential slots are gone; a Slack credential a saved graph still references on it is ignored and the run does not fail. The vault keeps `createdBy` / `updatedBy` ("Last changed by"); its tests moved to `test/credential-changer.test.ts` | [work and briefings](work-and-briefings.md#the-nightly-sync) |
| (this change) | Credentials list on a phone: at 390 px the fixed-width "Used by" and "Updated" columns left the credential's name 0 px wide and pushed the row past the card. The badges and the meta now sit on a second line under the name (a wrapper that is `display: contents` on a desktop, so the desktop row is unchanged), and the row's ⋯ menu shows without hovering | [mobile](mobile.md) |
| (this change) | LiteLLM replaces the agentgateway LLM example: the `llm-gateway` compose profile runs `v1.108.2-lts` pinned by digest (after the 21 October 2026 compromise; forbidden tags checked by a test and `deploy/scripts/verify-litellm-image.sh`) with its own `litellm` database in the infra Postgres, Ollama models on `/v1/messages` and `/v1/chat/completions`, and the UI on loopback only; the kustomize example in `landflow-dev` is a LiteLLM Deployment and database Job. `LANDFLOW_LLM_GATEWAY_ADMIN_PORT` is gone | [LLM gateway](llm-gateway.md#run-it) |
| (this change) | LiteLLM wave, LL02: the Anthropic API and OpenAI API credentials have a **Gateway** field (None or LiteLLM). Through LiteLLM, agents, the chat model nodes, the router and Spotlight (`litellm/<model>`) call the proxy with the virtual key, send ids that say who is calling (end user, tags, `in-process` spend-log metadata, trace id), send no fallback beta, list the gateway's models, and keep LiteLLM's call id and cost on the `ai_usage` row (`call_id`, `source`, `runtime`, `project_id`, `gateway_cost`) and the `ai.call` event. A spent LiteLLM budget shows as a usage cap. No credential field holds the master key: only `LANDFLOW_LLM_GATEWAY_ADMIN_KEY`. A gateway cost counts only for the server-configured gateway | [llm gateway](llm-gateway.md#point-landflow-at-it) |
| (this change) | Agents outside Landflow through the LLM gateway: a deployed kagent agent (own runtime or Claude Code) gets its own LiteLLM virtual key (budget = the agent's dollar limit, blocked when it is removed or moves runtime, never deleted), kagent's ModelConfig and the Secret's `api-key` point at the gateway (Anthropic format, or OpenAI for Ollama and OpenAI agents), the Claude Code sandbox's EgressPolicy gets the gateway's origin, and the leader's spend import copies their calls from LiteLLM's spend log into `ai_usage` once each (agent, project, person; `source = litellm`), so caps and Insights count them. The server's admin key (`LANDFLOW_LLM_GATEWAY_ADMIN_KEY`) is the only credential used; the limitation on remote agents' tokens narrows to agents off the gateway | [llm-gateway.md](llm-gateway.md#agents-outside-landflow), [a2a.md](a2a.md#landflow-agents-on-kagent) |
| (this change) | Budgets sync (LL04): Landflow's projects become LiteLLM teams, people customers and dollar caps backstop budgets a little above the cap (headroom 10%), idempotent and never deleting (what has no counterpart is listed as an orphan); run by Sync now, on a cap change and every 10 minutes while on; Settings → AI → LLM gateway card; uses only the server's `LANDFLOW_LLM_GATEWAY_URL` and `_ADMIN_KEY`; `backstopBudget` rounds without float noise | [llm gateway](llm-gateway.md#budgets-in-litellm) |
| (this change) | LiteLLM wave, LL05: asking for a higher AI cap. A person asks for a higher user, workflow or agent cap (`POST /api/ai/cap-requests`, table `ai_cap_requests`); the new nodes Cap Raise Request (`core.capRaiseTrigger`) and Apply Cap Raise (`core.applyCapRaise`) and the template `ai-cap-raise` run it through Ask a Person to the owners and admins other than the requester. Apply Cap Raise reads the server's own record of the run (not its input item), so only an approval by an active admin who is not the requester changes the cap; it then tells the budgets sync (`capsChanged('approval')`) and records what happened in LiteLLM (`gateway`). Also the wave's contract files, migrations 2200 to 2203 and variables; an admin turns the flow on once (`POST /api/ai/cap-requests/setup`) Review fixes: only the installed flow handles a request and applies a cap (no fallback to another workflow with the trigger; Apply checks the run is the installed flow's); a workflow cap is published before the request is approved, and the draft is restored if that fails; a stale approval never lowers or re-adds a cap; cancelling a request cancels its waiting run | [llm-gateway](llm-gateway.md#asking-for-a-higher-cap) |
| (this change) | LiteLLM wave, LL06: Runs → Insights → AI (`/insights/ai`) shows AI spend and tokens for 24 hours, 7 or 30 days by project, agent, person, model and source (calls imported from the gateway under "kagent" or "Claude Code on kagent"), every cap with a bar that turns amber at 80% and red at 100%, cap-raise requests with **Ask for more** (a dialog; admins get **Turn on cap requests**), and the gateway's health, models and, for admins, **Open LiteLLM**. A member sees only what they may open and their own calls, other people as "Teammates". `GET /api/insights/ai` and `GET /api/llm-gateway/status`; one card per row on a phone, tables on desktop | [the AI page](llm-gateway.md#the-ai-page) |
| (this change) | Wave L1 integration, from the live checks against LiteLLM `v1.108.2-lts` and Ollama: LiteLLM's spend log files a call under the response id (`chatcmpl-…`) and keeps the `x-litellm-call-id` in `litellm_call_id`, so the client and the spend import now match and count by that call id (before, Landflow never found its own calls and an imported row could not be matched to one). `/customer/update` ignores budget fields, so a person's removed cap left the LiteLLM budget in place: the budgets sync now changes a customer's budget through `/budget/update` on its budget object. The fake LiteLLM in the tests does both the way the proxy does. The dev gateway config gets `landflow-live-priced` (a priced alias of `qwen3-coder:30b`) for the live budget test. Also: Apply Cap Raise reads a finished run with `loadExecution`, the budgets sync is in the boot-order test, and the dependency baseline is refreshed (the existing runner/dispatch cycle, now reported through `runner/jobs.ts`) | [LLM gateway](llm-gateway.md#counting-each-call-once) |
| (this change) | LiteLLM live check on a production-profile host: three background calls (no run, no person) went through the gateway marked `in-process` but no usage scope metered them, so they were counted nowhere. A call outside a metered usage scope is now marked `unmetered`, and the spend import counts it, even on Landflow's own gateway key, as runtime Landflow. Spotlight's call was counted once, by call id | [LLM gateway](llm-gateway.md#counting-each-call-once) |
| (this change) | Wave L1 review fixes (Fable reviews of LL02 and of the integrated wave). The spend import no longer skips a row on anything a caller or a member controls: the `in-process` metadata and a credential's key are gone as reasons; a row is skipped only when its call id is already in `ai_usage`, and Landflow keeps a call id only from the server-configured gateway (`LANDFLOW_LLM_GATEWAY_URL`), so a credential pointing at another server can't answer with someone else's call id and erase it; a repeated call id no longer drops the usage row; embeddings through the gateway keep their call id too. Imported rows name no person, agent or project from the caller's headers or metadata (only a key Landflow minted, or the key's team), so a key holder can't charge another person's cap. The AI page shows Landflow's own background calls under Landflow. Also: the duplicated LL access rules in `auth/policy.ts`, the catalog's stale "a credential's Admin key wins", a doubled docs index entry, the roadmap's L1 section, the integration-test variables in development.md, and the cap-raise template's Slack advice | [LLM gateway](llm-gateway.md#counting-each-call-once) |
| (this change) | Wave L1 re-review fix: a caller can send its own `x-litellm-call-id`, so pairing by call id alone let a key holder reuse the call id of a call Landflow counted and hide their own. Migration 2204 adds `ai_usage.gateway_request_id` (unique) and `gateway_key_hash`; Landflow records the hash of the key for calls to the server-configured gateway (Anthropic, OpenAI chat and embeddings), and the spend import names each row by LiteLLM's `request_id` and lets a Landflow row claim exactly one spend row with the same call id and key. A reused call id, or the same call id under another key, is now imported. The trusted-gateway check compares scheme, host, port and path. Docs list the remaining gaps: a caller-named end user still spends that person's LiteLLM backstop, a self-metering plugin that calls the gateway is counted twice, and a call logged more than about 15 minutes late can be missed. |
| (this change) | Wave L1, second re-review fix: pairing by call id and key still let a member whose own key is in a Landflow credential reuse the call id of a Landflow call and hide a call of theirs (when the import read theirs first). Landflow now keeps the id the server's gateway gave the response (the body's `id`, a stream's first chunk, Anthropic's `message_start`; verified against LiteLLM `v1.108.2-lts` to be the spend row's `request_id`) as `gateway_request_id`, and the import skips exactly that spend row. The call id pairs only a call without a response id (embeddings) on the server's own key, which only the server holds. When the import reads a spend row before the meter records the call, the meter takes the imported row over (one row, Landflow's attribution). The fake gateway now answers with response ids as LiteLLM does; the live test checks all four call paths and that the import adds nothing for them. |
| (this change) | Wave L1, third review (approved) follow-ups: no response id is kept from a LiteLLM response-cache hit (`x-litellm-cache-key`), and the docs say the cache stays off; a meter takeover of an imported row never lowers its cost; a stream's first non-empty chunk id is the response id; the server-key comment no longer claims Landflow mints every call id. Tests for the cache hit and the takeover's cost. |
| (this change) | B35 (wave 6, closes CH17): one Spotlight for the whole app. ⌘K (⌘I an alias) opens a shell panel on every page (360 px, drag to resize; a bottom sheet with three heights on a phone), mounted once in the layout so the conversation stays between pages. A chip says what it does: "Building <flow>" on the editor (its own column stays the builder and takes the focus; no second panel), "Asking the brain" elsewhere, "About <thing>" after the `landflow:ask-about` event, with a way to the other mode. The Brain page's docked Ask panel and sheet are gone (its Ask button opens the shell panel, the open page is the chip); QuickOpen moved from ⌘K to ⌘P. The rail has an "Ask or build… ⌘K" button. The editor's first ⌘K now lands the caret once its folded column has opened. | [spotlight](spotlight.md#one-spotlight) |
| (this change) | B35 review fixes: on the editor the brain panel and the builder never show together (the builder folds to its strip while the brain panel shows; ⌘K, the button or the strip bring it back); a link in an answer on the Brain page opens in the workbench; closing the panel or removing the subject chip ends about mode; ask-about events with a non-mention type are refused; Ctrl is not the key on a Mac; the mode chip menu closes on an outside click. | [spotlight](spotlight.md#one-spotlight) |
| (this change) | Ask about it (B36, CH16): an **Ask Spotlight** item on the ⋯ menu of workflows, runs, agents, data tables, credentials and brain pages, and an **Ask why it failed** button on a failed run (list row, one-line row, run page, preview). Each fires one `landflow:ask-about` event with the resource and three starter questions for its type; a credential sends only its name and id. The panel that listens is B35. | [Ask about it](spotlight.md#ask-about-it) |
| (this change) | B37, V3 on the web: a "Your morning" card at the top of Home and the Brain index shows your own briefing (nightly text or live, Yesterday, Related with a reason chip, lists folded after three, links only to this instance or GitHub), a Delivery line with a None / Slack / Email menu that writes `briefingDelivery`, and a quiet "Set up tomorrow sync" for admins that creates the workflow from the template and opens it unpublished. "What finished" and a live "Slack is set up" check need a route change and are left for later. | [page](work-and-briefings.md#on-the-web) |
| (this change) | B38, the brain side of the mod: a Work overlap detector that introduces two people whose org work of the last 3 days is about the same thing (one suggestion addressed to both, org titles only, never from private or team work, once in 14 days, "Don't suggest this again" mutes it for that person; no Slack message on its own); the mod's `brain_ask` tool (`POST /api/brain/ask` with the key, the answer quoted as data); and `landflow-brain` and `landflow-harness` in the repository's `.claude-plugin/marketplace.json`. | [second-brain](second-brain.md#work-overlap), [mod](landflow-brain-mod.md#brain_ask) |
| (this change) | Wave 6 integration fix: B38 listed the two Claude Code mods (`landflow-brain`, `landflow-harness`) in the repository marketplace, and `plugin-flows.test.ts` read every entry as a node plugin. The test now checks the node plugins under `./plugins/` as before and the mods as mods: no warnings, no node plugin, no flows, their hooks reported as not used by Landflow. |
| (this change) | Wave 6 carryover from the wave 5 and L1 reviews: a build prompt counts as a change, and a Slack Apply click on one is refused (Slack never showed the button, and a forged click made a workflow outside Build it); Build it's `?ask=` sends only on an empty canvas and shares one draft function with the composer; a suggestion with a draft and a build prompt describes the draft; the apply route's `createdWorkflowId` link and a real GitHub fixture through the work-github template are tested; the rail's **All projects** forgets the remembered project and **Show all projects** takes `?project=` out of the URL; Explore drops a category its source lacks; the empty Credentials page's button is primary and a failed project read says so; the Workflows list's last run and New menu move to `list-parts.tsx`; the Tomorrow sync's setup says a member's change stops the nightly run; SQLite sets its busy timeout before WAL (the "database is locked" flakes). What waits is in the roadmap. | [roadmap](roadmap.md#company-backbone-left-open-from-the-wave-5-and-litellm-l1-reviews-2026-11-15) |
| (this change) | Wave 6 integration fix (B35 x B36): an ask-about about another thing than the panel is about now starts a new conversation in the shell panel, so **Ask why it failed** and **Ask Spotlight** show their three starters every time and the first question sends none of the earlier, unrelated turns as history. The same thing again, or an answer still coming, keeps the conversation. A test fires the event through the listener with a non-empty conversation. | [Ask about it](spotlight.md#ask-about-it) |
| (this change) | Wave 6 review polish: run refs read "<workflow> run" as the @ picker names them; ⌘K is the composer hint; ⌘K does not open the panel behind a modal dialog; a flow briefing with no summary shows no "Live" line; the ask-about event name has one definition; the work-overlap tests cover an owner outside the pair; docs corrected for the ask-about panel, the brain mod, work visibility, held-back numbers and new limitations. |

## 2026-11-14: Landledger

| Commit | What it built | Documented in |

| (this change) | Token spend wave 12, exact counts per part (SP22): a call's split is counted with Anthropic's count endpoint (cumulative prefixes, cached, budgeted per credential), OpenAI's encodings offline or an Ollama model's own vocabulary (a byte-level BPE with the `qwen2` and `llama-bpe` pre-tokenizers, loaded once per model digest), and `method` says which; `formatting` is the reported input minus the counted parts; a gateway split with the call's response id is replaced; `LANDFLOW_AI_PARTS_COUNT` and `LANDFLOW_AI_PARTS_COUNT_PER_MINUTE`; the shared BPE fixture and its script. | [llm-gateway.md](llm-gateway.md#what-a-prompt-was-made-of), [limitations.md](limitations.md), [roadmap.md](roadmap.md) |
| (this change) | Wave 12, the gateway's split (SP21): a LiteLLM callback (`deploy/llm-gateway/custom_callbacks.py`) splits each call kagent's agents and Claude Code make, counts the parts with the model's tokenizer (an Ollama vocabulary, tiktoken, Anthropic's count endpoint or an estimate) and posts numbers and keyed hashes to the public, signed `POST /api/gateway-parts` under the gateway's response id; the spend import fills the split's `usage_id` in either order and a Landflow split wins; compose and the k8s example mount it, off until `LANDFLOW_LLM_GATEWAY_PARTS_SECRET` is set; Python tests run in the pinned image | [llm-gateway.md](llm-gateway.md#the-gateways-split), [limitations.md](limitations.md#the-gateways-split-wave-12) |
| (this change) | Wave 12 review fixes, the gateway's split (SP21): the callback strips `/api/chat` and `/v1/messages` from the `api_base` LiteLLM logs before asking `/api/show` and the count endpoint (it asked `/api/chat/api/show`, so no call got its own tokenizer); a /v1/messages call to a non-Anthropic model keeps its system prompt (LiteLLM leaves it only in `input`); failed tokenizer loads are remembered; a 4xx is not retried; warnings once; counting runs on the callback's own bounded pool; a call with tool definitions on a Claude model was an `estimate` (commit 0ee707ca, next row, reversed that); `gateway-parts` joins the public rate-limit groups; a split that waited for a row the meter wrote first is attached; the tests drive real `litellm.acompletion` and `anthropic_messages` calls against a loopback fake, and the fixture is SP22's. |
| (this change) | Wave 12, the gateway's split, Claude counts (SP21, commit 0ee707ca): a Claude call's tool definitions go through the count endpoint's `tools` field and its system prompt through `system`, so they are counted by the provider (`provider`, not `estimate`); the deployment's key is read from the router when LiteLLM's log of a `/v1/messages` call lacks it, and the environment's `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` is used only when the model has no custom `api_base`, so it never goes to another host; a 429 from Landflow is retried after its `Retry-After` (at most 5 s) and dropped with its own warning after three tries. | [llm-gateway](llm-gateway.md#the-gateways-split) |
| (this change) | Wave 12 fixes after the SP21 review: the callback's tests cover a 429 (three tries, `Retry-After` honoured up to 5 s, nothing raised) and a 429 followed by a success; the web tests check `/api/gateway-parts` gets 429 with `Retry-After` past `LANDFLOW_RATE_LIMIT_PUBLIC` in the production profile and no limit in development; `/api/gateway-parts` is named in the `LANDFLOW_RATE_LIMIT_PUBLIC` description and the limiter's header; `llm-gateway.md` says a 4xx other than 429 is not retried, that operators with many agents should raise the limit, and that a gateway container made before the callback mount must be recreated, not restarted; the unused `_stubs` is gone. | [llm-gateway](llm-gateway.md#the-gateways-split), [configuration](reference/configuration.md) |
| (this change) | Cache fixes (SP19, wave 12, D8): the Anthropic cache point sits after the stable system prompt (persona, skills, project block, practices) and the memory, intro line and date line follow it as a second system block; the conversation's last block gets a breakpoint (three of four); memory is read once per run; `prefixHash` is now the stable prefix's hash. Before/after on a fake Anthropic cache (`prompt-cache-layout.test.ts`; a four-iteration run with a `remember`, then a second run four minutes later): base's layout read 3,215 cache tokens on iterations 2 to 4 and paid 373 at the full input price, and the second run read 1,612; the new layout read 5,054 and paid 0 at full price on iterations 2 to 4, and 3,180 on the second run. At Sonnet prices the first run went from $0.01541 to $0.00941 and the second from $0.00737 to $0.00208. |
| (this change) | Token spend wave 12, SP05: `GET /api/executions/:id/spend` (a run's totals, slices, composition, cache line and calls, sub-runs the viewer can't open counted in `hidden`) and `GET /api/ai-calls/:id` (one call, its split from Landflow or the gateway, the previous call, the run link); `/api/ai-calls` is read like executions; the demo run's numbers are a test fixture. No screen yet | [run-trace.md](run-trace.md#spend), [reference/api.md](reference/api.md) |
| (this change) | Token spend wave 12, SP06: the Run Trace's Spend tab (`components/run-trace/spend.tsx`, `lib/client/run-spend.ts`, `lib/shared/run-spend.ts` copied from SP05's contract): totals with unpriced and hidden-run lines, by purpose / model / node / agent / step, the cache line with miss reasons, the prompt composition bar with its count method, and the calls list; shown for a run with model tokens, refreshed every 10 s while live; a call row opens the journal span it ended (calls are matched to spans by model, agent and end time), the tab reads once more when the run ends, and a failed read has a Try again button. Checked live on made-up data through intercepted routes (SP05's route is not merged yet): desktop light and dark, no console errors, no sideways scroll at 390 px. |
| (this change) | Wave 12, SP07: a model step's sheet in the Run Trace shows what the call was made of (one bar, each part's tokens and share, the cached prefix marked, the method in words, "From the LLM gateway" for a gateway split), the cache line ("8,000 read from the cache · 2,000 not cached · written: 0") and what changed against the previous call ("Same prefix as the previous call", "The instructions changed"), or "No split for this call" with the reason; the step's Tokens row names cache reads and writes, and an agent session's header says "N cached". A team report's estimated cost for Anthropic members has been cache-aware since wave 11 (it prices the session's cache reads and writes); the cached tokens now show beside it. `matchSpanToCall` follows `run-spend-api.md`. Checked in a browser against intercepted spend routes (SP05's not merged yet). Review fixes: the sheet matches a step to a call by node as well, says "Couldn't read this call's spend." when a request fails, and names the 500-call limit; the badge reads "cached prefix" again. |
| (this change) | Wave 12 carryover, join tests (constitution lesson 7): `spend-cache-join` runs two real Run an Agent runs (three loop steps with a `remember`, then a second run) through the real Anthropic adapter to SP19's fake prompt cache while the host's listener counts each split through SP22's fake count endpoint, and SP05's routes read them back: three cache points at most, every split `provider`-counted and summing to the input, one prefix hash across both runs, memory's hash moving and the instructions' not, a hit rate with no miss, "same prefix" on step 2, no prompt word anywhere. `spend-trace-join` reads one real run (the AI Agent node's three steps, then a Guardrails node with its own chat model) through the real journal translator and SP05's routes: every model span matches exactly one call through SP07's `matchSpanToCall`, and SP06's tab and SP07's call view render from the routes' answers. Under the new `LANDFLOW_INTEGRATION=gateway-image` flag (the pinned LiteLLM image by digest, `--network none`; `test/gateway-image.ts`), `spend-bpe-join` has the callback's Python BPE and `hash_segments` agree with SP22's counter and `hashParts` on the fixture, 200 seeded strings and one prompt under one key, and `spend-gateway-join` posts the callback's own signed bytes for an Anthropic-shaped call to `POST /api/gateway-parts`, pairs them through the spend import, shows them in `GET /api/ai-calls/:id`, and has a Landflow split win in all three orders with no failed write. The trace join found that Run an Agent writes no model span per step, so its calls have no sheet (a limitation and a roadmap line). The roadmap folds wave 11's five headings into one section, puts SP22 under wave 12, and lists what stays open from the wave 11 reviews | [development](development.md#integration-tests-landflow_integration), [limitations](limitations.md), [roadmap](roadmap.md) |
| (this change) | Wave 12 security fix: the production check refuses the gateway's public development values `landflow-dev-parts-only` (`LANDFLOW_LLM_GATEWAY_PARTS_SECRET`) and `sk-landflow-dev-master-only` (the gateway key variables); neither contains `dev-only`, so the check missed them; a test names the variables and never the values. | [deployment](deployment.md#production-profile), [llm-gateway](llm-gateway.md) |
| (this change) | Wave 12 review fixes, run spend (SP05): `GET /api/ai-calls/:id` applies the report's child-session rule (a call in a child session of an agent the viewer cannot read answers as not found); the previous call is looked up with the agent in the SQL filter, so more than 200 newer rows of other agents on the same model no longer hide it; the cache line and a call's `previous` compare hashes only between splits of one source (a gateway and a Landflow split are keyed differently); tests cover each, and a run from before wave 11 degrading to its totals; `limitations.md` names the single 1,024-token cache minimum. | [limitations](limitations.md), [run-trace](run-trace.md#spend) |
| (this change) | Wave 12 review fixes, the Run Trace's spend screens (SP06, SP07): the Spend tab and the call sheet pair a model span with its call by one rule (`lib/client/span-call-match.ts`: model, node, agent and time window, one call to one span, an open span ends at its start), so the row that opens a span is the call the sheet shows; one set of count-method words on both ("Counted by the provider", "Counted with the model's own tokenizer", "Estimated from text length"); the composition note counts chat calls only and no longer says every missing split was built outside Landflow; the parts that lead a cached request read **stable prefix** and the conversation reads **may be cached** (the conversation's last block is a cache point since SP19); the tab and every sheet share one read of `GET /api/executions/:id/spend`; a hidden-runs line without calls shows no `$0`; the sheet's "no cache numbers" line goes by provider family (`anthropic/…`, `litellm`); two mis-indented blocks formatted; the stale SP06 roadmap sentence removed. | [run-trace](run-trace.md#spend), [roadmap](roadmap.md) |
| (this change) | Wave 12 review fixes, cache layout (SP19): `limitations.md` says "before this change" for the plain input price and names that, with the prompt cache on, a one-shot Anthropic call's string last message goes as a text block with `cache_control` and pays the cache-write price; `agents.md` says the measured agent has no project block and four node tools plus `remember`; the layout test that rewrites the request back to base's shape is titled for what it does; the `// SP19:` tag in `ai.ts` is part of the JSDoc; the `N cached` sentence is wrapped. | [limitations](limitations.md), [agents](agents.md#prompt-cache) |
| (this change) | Wave 12 carryover docs (commit 3dab2279): the roadmap has one section per token spend wave (wave 11 as one section with a bullet per package, wave 12 in package order, SP22 moved into it), a section for what stays open from the wave 11 reviews, and SP05's stale "no screen yet" sentence is fixed; `limitations.md` names that Run an Agent writes no model span per loop step; the long lines of the SP19, SP05 and SP07 docs are wrapped to the files' width. | [roadmap](roadmap.md), [limitations](limitations.md) |
| (this change) | `gpt-tokenizer` is pinned at exactly 4.1.2 in `apps/web/package.json` (and the lockfile's specifier): the count's cache-off fix relies on its `setMergeCacheSize`, which a later version may change; no other dependency changed. | [llm-gateway](llm-gateway.md#what-a-prompt-was-made-of) |
| (this change) | A call's "what changed" line says nothing when the two calls' splits can't be compared (one has none, or one is the gateway's and one Landflow's): `previous.compared` is `false` then, where the sheet used to say "The prefix changed since the previous call". The wave 12 fix pass had stopped comparing such pairs but left the line claiming a change. |
| (this change) | Wave 12 re-review fixes, spend reads (SP06, SP07): a finished run never keeps a mid-run spend answer (the shared read records whether the run was live when it was asked, and a tab or sheet opened after the end reads once more); a read asked during one in flight is followed by one more, so the end-of-run read is not lost to a poll; one poll per run for the tab and the sheets; a model step's sheet pairs its call against the whole run's spans and polls by the whole run's state, not the replay cut; `previous.compared` is also false when a split has no hashes to compare; config tests cover `LANDFLOW_LLM_GATEWAY_KEY` and that the master key is never printed; `deployment.md` says the host cannot check the gateway's own master key variable; over-wide lines wrapped. | [run-trace](run-trace.md#spend), [deployment](deployment.md#production-profile) |
| (this change) | Limitations: on Ollama a call's `formatting` part holds the model's chat template and its own tool-calling instructions, about half of a call with tools on qwen3-coder:30b in the wave 12 live check (221 of 355 tokens). |
| (this change) | Spotlight scenario suite (SB00, wave sb1): the evaluation's builds, one-step fixes and agent asks as scenarios with a scripted and a live form (`apps/web/test/spotlight-scenarios/`, `spotlight-scenarios.test.ts`), the Build, Fix one step and Agents scorecards under `LANDFLOW_INTEGRATION=spotlight`, and `local-only.cjs`, a guard that makes any connection or lookup outside this machine fail. Review fixes: a scenario's script is one scripted model across the first message and every session reply, a session's fix request carries the failed run's digests as the editor's `runs` do, the live form builds against the bundled Linear and Messaging plugins (not stand-ins), `runs-right` checks the If's condition against the pinned items and the agent being run once, the live runner needs the guard from the process's start, the scorecard prints a time range and the skipped scenarios, and its JSON keeps an error's kind, not its text. The scenario data directory has no exit handler (the host flushes `insights.json` on exit; removing the directory first ended the live run with ENOENT): stale `landflow-spotlight-scenarios-*` directories older than an hour are removed at the next import. | [development](development.md#spotlight-scorecard), [assistant](assistant.md#how-spotlight-is-measured) |
| (this change) | SB01, the first draft fills its collections: Ollama gets `format: "json"` and the schema in the system prompt (the schema-held reply left every list empty in 9 of 9 drafts); the reply is parsed with zod and sent back once when it does not parse; the draft is checked (`emptyParts`: empty lists, collection shapes, wiring, hollow steps) and sent back once with a line per empty part, the second draft taken when it keeps the first's step types and is no worse; what is still empty comes as `FlowDraft.left` (`lib/shared/draft-left.ts`); a near-miss step type (`core.linear.linear`) maps to the one type it can mean; test items pin on an app trigger or on the app step that reads data after a schedule. |
| (this change) | Spotlight wave sb1, SB02: the first draft gets the chat's guards (`draft-guards.ts`): made-up channels, tables, owners, addresses, ids and URL hosts cleared and listed, one trigger, no empty Set, credentials listed; `POST /api/ai/build` takes `workflowId` so a workflow's allowed node types apply; `withoutInventedIds` widened to destination-like keys and URL hosts; the reply says "Left for you: …". Docs: [assistant.md](assistant.md). |
| (this change) | Test and fix cannot wreck a flow (SB03, wave sb1, finding 1.2): `needsPerson` reads a host's outbound block (`Blocked: …`) before the credential rules and the session ends with a *blocked* **Needs you** row, no fix asked; the yes to sending is per workflow and destination (`destinationsOf`), asked before every run, with a *destination* row and **Undo that change** when a fix brings in a new one; a fix that would remove a step the goal names or change a URL, address, channel or id the person gave is not applied (`givenKept`, `givenValuesKept` in the chat route, `PERSON_ONLY` and the session prompts say the same); a pass after a fix is reviewed before **Ready for production**, never after removed steps; asks-first sessions and auto fixes that remove a step wait for Apply (D1, `spotlight-autonomy.ts`); `publishToProduction` calls `notePublished`. Tests: `run-check-destinations`, `spotlight-session-safety`, `given-values`; scenarios `sb03-*`. |
| (this change) | Spotlight: a question is answered, not applied (`askKind`: no `propose_changes` for a question, nothing applied, a **Fix it** bubble after the answer), and Spotlight acts alone only while it builds a brand-new draft (`spotlight-autonomy.ts`, decision D1): a saved or published workflow shows the card and waits for Apply, with a one-line note in the panel (under the switch row, never over the conversation), and a removal waits for a click even on a new draft (SB04, wave sb1). Review fixes: conditions and comma requests ("when it fails, retry 3 times") are changes, not questions; publishing from the tab by any route ends autonomy (the editor's `active` flag); "X and fix it" and subject-less statements read as changes; the Fix it bubble's rule is documented and tested. | [assistant.md](assistant.md#autonomous-mode) |
| (this change) | A new agent survives a send-back (SB05): its name is recorded as proposed only when its proposal is held, so a send-back no longer drops it as a stand-in; `newAgent` takes `tools` and `knowledge` (through `/api/agents/design` to the architect's request and brief); "after X" places it after X; an Ollama tool-call parse error is retried once (`withParseRetry`). | [assistant.md](assistant.md#agents-and-teams) |
| (this change) | SB06: the Agent architect on a local model. On an Ollama model the dialog is offered the flat `propose_design` in place of `propose_agent` (its nested arrays failed in Ollama's tool-call parser, so 7 of 7 tries ended without a draft), which grew name, audience, mission, Always/Never rules, answer length and workflow and agent tool ids as plain strings; a parse error is retried with it whatever the provider. |
| (this change) | Wave sb1 merge fixes: the scenario harness wires `start.autonomy` (asks-first leaves a card waiting, auto applies what needs no click, a session waiting for Apply is stopped) and `start.approvedDestinations` (SB03 and SB04 had no harness on their base), compares a session's canvas as the editor gives it back, spends no script turn on the fake Ollama's model list and answers a throwing turn as the server's error; SB02's build scripts answer SB01's send-back with the same draft, SB00's harness build gives its Set a field (SB01 sends an empty one back, SB02 removes it), SB04's status call points at the echo server, SB03's destination scenario asks without URLs and fixes the step that failed, and its asks-first scenario checks the canvas, not "no card"; a session fix that swaps a URL the person gave for a host nobody gave is told "Only the person can change that" (SB03's check reads the ops before SB02's made-up-values strip); SB05's chat parse retry reads Ollama's detail and no longer retries a 500 that says what it was (out of memory). | [development](development.md#spotlight-scorecard) |
| (this change) | Wave sb1 carryover, join tests (constitution lesson 7; `spotlight-sb1-joins.test.ts`): `sb1-autonomy-join` runs the real `autonomous.ts`, `test-session.ts`, `spotlight-autonomy.ts` and the reply's `holdsForClick` with the real `applyChanges`: on a saved workflow a typed change sends no `auto`, `afterReply` applies nothing and Test and fix waits for the card's Apply (then runs again) or Dismiss (then the outcome card); on a new draft the session applies a parameter fix and a removal waits with its reason; after `publishToProduction` the draft asks first. The draft join runs `POST /api/ai/build` on a fake Ollama: SB01's send-back fills the If, SB02 keeps one trigger, drops the empty Set (and SB01's empty item for it), clears a made-up channel and URL host and lists them with the credential in one `left` (no duplicates); with a workflow's allow-list the model's catalog lacks the left-out type. The chat destination join: a fix session's swap to httpbin or to another local port is not shown and the model hears why. The question × session join: the session's real fix and review requests reach the chat with `propose_changes` offered, and a typed question sends no session. The new agent join: `POST /api/agents/design` hands SB05's tools and knowledge to SB06's first request (only `propose_design` offered), a Linear node tool is kept, and an Ollama parse error still ends with a designed agent. SB04's scenarios read SB00's Bug triage fixture instead of an inlined copy; `spotlight-spend-parts.test.ts` checks a Spotlight chat call and an architect call on Ollama each write their `ai_call_parts` row (the wave 11 live check, in unit form). | [development](development.md#spotlight-scorecard) |
| (this change) | Roadmap, Spotlight wave sb1: what is owed after the merge (the wave's live checks on Ollama under `local-only.cjs`, and for a person the asks-first wording on a real phone and when "saved" ends autonomy). | [roadmap](roadmap.md#spotlight-wave-sb1) |
| (this change) | SB05 review fixes (wave sb1): the parse retry's tests throw the adapter's real shape (`NodeOperationError('Ollama error', { description: 'XML syntax error …' })`), a 500 that says what it was (out of memory) is asked once, the usage-cap case has a message that matches the parse pattern, the `assistant.retry` span event carries message and description (cut to 200), and `assistant.md` names the patterns retried. | [assistant](assistant.md) |
| (this change) | Wave sb1 review fixes, destinations and made-up values: a URL is read with `new URL` (`urlHost`): a backslash or an unparseable URL is an unknown host (cleared and listed as "an address that cannot be read" in the guards, `{{ … }}` in a destination), `https://evil.com#@good.com/x` and `?@` are evil.com, `https://evil.com{{ $json.path }}` is judged by evil.com; `propertyType` reads the top-level property first; `namedAs` drops "for" and "from" as cues, adds "by", "with" and "as", and never counts a kind word or the app's name; an expression destination is asked again before every run and never kept (`approvedOnce`); an empty URL is "an address not filled in yet", a **Needs you** row (`what: 'address'`) that opens the step before any run; the autonomous prompt keeps a value unless the message asks for the change; `PERSON_ONLY` reads "blocked by this host" / outbound policy, not the bare word; the panel's title is "Needs you: apply the fix?" while a fix waits for Apply. | [assistant](assistant.md#test-and-fix), [limitations](limitations.md) |
| (this change) | Wave sb1 review fixes, autonomy and the draft: a test session sends its autonomy (`auto` or `asks-first`, validated by the chat route, default asks-first) and the assistant's session prompt and `propose_changes` result say what follows (an asks-first fix is a card that waits for Apply, never "applied by itself"); `needsAClick` also names turning a step off (`turns off “X”`; the `spotlight-autonomy.ts` contract copy and `autonomy.md` changed together), so under `auto` a turn-off waits for Apply as the docs say; the draft's reply says "Testing it now" only where `autonomyOf` is `auto`; a dropped trigger's pin moves to the kept trigger only where the builder would have pinned (`pinsOnTrigger`), never onto a schedule; `limitations.md` says `givenKept` reads only top-level string parameters. | [assistant](assistant.md#test-and-fix), [limitations](limitations.md) |
| (this change) | Wave sb1 review fixes, the first draft and the architect: the builder's reply is read leniently (a wrong-typed `name`, `testData` or `output` is ignored, a numeric `key` is text), a remapped step type is listed as left, a workflow trigger's `inputs`, a form's `formFields` and a schedule rule of a named kind are not sent back, the timeout message names the configured `LANDFLOW_BUILDER_TIMEOUT_SECONDS`, and a failed send-back keeps the first draft (tested); `askKind` reads revert, undo, restore and roll back as changes and the asks-first note says "saved or published"; `placedAsAsked` counts an `addNode` after the agent as onward and leaves an op with an `output` alone; the Agent architect reuses `isParseError` (a usage cap and an out-of-memory 500 are not retried), has no "Designed for Spotlight" summary and drops "none", "no", "n/a" and "-" from the rules; its over-wide lines are wrapped. Docs: the roadmap's wave sb1 bullets are one style, `development.md` and `agents.md` are rewrapped. | [assistant](assistant.md), [agents](agents.md#agent-architect) |
| (this change) | Wave sb1 re-review fixes, blocking: "Needs you: apply the fix?" is the title only while a card of a reply the session asked for (its `testId`) is open (`sessionWaitsForApply`), not for a held card of an autonomous typed change; the one-run yes for an expression destination is per step (`<workflow> <step> {{ … }}`, the steps the row lists, `Need.nodes`), so a step added after the row was shown asks on its own (superseded by the next row: the yes is cleared when a session ends). | [assistant](assistant.md#test-and-fix) |
| (this change) | Wave sb1 re-review fixes, hardening: a literal host followed straight by an expression (`https://good.com{{ '.evil.com' }}`) is an unknown host and a port expression (`evil.com:{{ $json.port }}`) is judged by its host; a destination with an expression in its host shows its literal start ("evil.com{{ … }}"); `urlHost` reads a path ("/api/x") as no host; the made-up values guard takes an existing node's type for its app's name (`typeOf`); the scenario env's stale cleanup keeps a directory whose recorded process still runs (`pid` file); the remap row of a near-miss type is listed only for a node kept; `pinsOnTrigger`'s comment is in its place; docs: `agents.md` paragraph, "removes a step, turns one off or leaves", the chat route's `session` and `autonomy` in `assistant.md` and `reference/api.md`; the architect's over-wide lines wrapped. | [assistant](assistant.md), [agents](agents.md#agent-architect) |
| (this change) | Wave sb1 second re-check fixes: a once-yes for an expression destination is keyed by the step's id and its exact URL text, covers the one run that follows, and is cleared when the session ends (a changed expression, or a step deleted and added back, asks again); a row that asks about an expression destination names every expression destination so one yes covers them all; "apply the fix?" is not the title while the reply is still streaming (`sessionIsWaiting`); `=https://evil.com/{{ … }}` is judged by its host; an expression in the user name (`https://{{ $json.u }}@evil.com/x`) is judged by the host after the last `@`; a Stop during agent design starts no run; `//evil.com/x` has the host evil.com. | [assistant](assistant.md#test-and-fix) |
| (this change) | Wave sb1 final fixes: the destination check runs before every real run, the first too (a URL that changes while the agents settle, e.g. an older proposal card applied, asks before it runs); a user-name expression (`https://{{ $json.u }}@good.com/x`) shows its host but asks every run (`good.com{{ … }}`), as a value with `/`, `?`, `#` or `@` moves the host; an `@` inside `{{ }}` makes the host unreadable (client and the made-up values guard); `= https://…` with a space reads as the URL it is; after a yes on an expression row the card says it covers the next run only. | [assistant.md](assistant.md#test-and-fix) |
| (this change) | Wave sb1 live checks recorded in the roadmap (production profile, Ollama, headed Chrome, under `local-only.cjs`, nothing refused): the incident replayed, the question, first drafts, the Agent architect on the local model, and what is left for wave sb2. | [roadmap.md](roadmap.md) |

| (this change) | SB13's small review notes: a plan step's `attach` must be a connection its node supplies (`subnodeProblems`, the plan route's repair and so the build route's 400; a loader attached as a chat model, or Slack as one, is refused; an action node may attach as `ai_tool`); the plan card item gives the builder chat a line with its steps (`historyOf`, was "(no reply)"); tests for the build route's 400 on a sub-node main step and for `withoutStep` removing an attached sub-node with its parent; style: the dead `export { withoutStep }` and the `// SB13:` import marker in `plan-edit.ts` go, the mis-indented test line is fixed | [assistant.md](assistant.md#building-a-flow-in-stages) |
| (this change) | More of the sb3 packages' small review notes: `REPOSITORY` no longer reads pass/fail, read/write, PRs/MRs, go/no-go, true/false or on/off as a repository (test in `agent-brief.test.ts`); SB15's hollow-step test now sets the condition and checks the row closes; limitations says a default value counts as not filled; the editor comment on `spotlightWorks` says it covers the chat's agent rows too | [limitations.md](limitations.md), [agents.md](agents.md#building-an-agent) |
| (this change) | The scripted Spotlight scenarios that the merge left on the old shape pass again (7 of them timed out on `No scripted turn left` at the integration head): `sb15-linear-summary` and `sb15-nothing-left` script a plan's steps (`start.plan`, one answer each) now that a build plans first, and `sb16-pr-reviewer-empty`, `sb16-agent-on-canvas` and `sb18-*` script the architect's one more call for test cases (SB17), which answers none so the eval gate has nothing to run | Found by the gate's last command (`spotlight-scenarios.test.ts`); the same 7 failed on the integration head |
| (this change) | `spotlight-build-summary.test.ts`'s build-route case no longer needs a model: the route plans first without a `plan`, and that call went to whatever Ollama the machine had (it passed alone, with a real model, and failed with 502 in the full run); the body now carries a plan, so the route goes straight to the recorded builder | Found by the gate's first command (`pnpm check`) |
| (this change) | Next.js 16.4.3 → 16.4.6 (`apps/web/package.json`, lockfile): fixes the moderate advisory GHSA-mfr8-8qww-v76h (root params disclosed across a `use cache` function called through `unstable_cache()`, next 16.3.0–16.4.4), which also made `next build` stop after compiling on its security check | `docs/history.md` |
| (this change) | The staged build knows what a team node writes on its Needs a person output: `core.runTeam`'s sample now has `reason`, so a step on output 1 that reads `{{ $json.reason }}` is not sent back (the other agent nodes have one output and their fields were already complete). Test in `flow-builder-staged.test.ts`; `limitations.md` lists it. | `docs/assistant.md`, `docs/limitations.md` |
| (this change) | When planning used the whole time budget, the staged build no longer returns a draft of steps that each say "ran out of time": with no call made it fails with "planning used the whole time budget" (as it fails when every call fails). `assistant.md` says which paths share the one budget (`buildPlanFirst`: the build route without a plan and the brain writer) and that the editor's plan card and build are two requests with a budget each. Test in `flow-builder-staged.test.ts`. | `docs/assistant.md` |
| (this change) | The brain writer no longer rebuilds a draft for a request whose suggestion is still open or snoozed (same fingerprint): it keeps the saved draft and the finding does not count against `MAX_DRAFTS_PER_PASS`. `second-brain.md` and `limitations.md` now say what a pass really costs (unmet demand already reports at most 3 requests). Test in `brain-writer-drafts.test.ts`. | `docs/second-brain.md`, `docs/limitations.md` |
| (this change) | Scenario and test fixes from the sb3 re-review: live `sb15-linear-summary` and `sb15-nothing-left` plan for real (the plan is the unit form's first scripted answer, not `start.plan`); the build-route summary test uses `staged: false` instead of a mismatched plan; the agent card's subscription test gets one agent id per Create so three steps share one subscription. | `docs/assistant.md` |
| (this change) | `repairPlan` says how many `assumptions` or `cannot` lines it left out past the 30-line limit (the last of the 30 slots) instead of dropping them silently; `assistant.md` states the limit. Test in `build-plan.test.ts`. `roadmap.md` notes the older `agentUsedBy` gaps (a scheduled or four-eyes pending publish containing the step; another agent delegating to this one). | `docs/assistant.md`, `docs/roadmap.md` |
| (this change) | The brain keeps an open suggestion's draft only while the prompt it was built from is the same: the draft is saved with a hash of that prompt (`draftPromptHash`, in the suggestion's JSON), a changed prompt (unmet demand's example requests leave the 30-day window) rebuilds it and the rebuild counts against `MAX_DRAFTS_PER_PASS`, and only a `detector` suggestion of the same detector and fingerprint is reused. Tests in `brain-writer-drafts.test.ts`. The docs no longer say to dismiss a suggestion for a new draft (dismissing holds the request back); `assistant.md`'s plan-lines sentences are one; `limitations.md` says a step on the team node's Done output reading `$json.reason` is not flagged. | `docs/second-brain.md`, `docs/limitations.md`, `docs/assistant.md` |
| (this change) | The live pass of Spotlight wave sb3 on Claude (`anthropic/claude-sonnet-5-5`, production profile, headed Chrome): B1's plan card, build, summary card and credential picker read in the browser (largest prompt 4,257 tokens, nine calls, $0.0739), 18 scenarios on the scorecard, and the pass stopped when the Anthropic account reached its usage limit; what was and was not read, the moved checks with their causes and what is left are in the roadmap. Docs only. | [roadmap.md](roadmap.md#spotlight-wave-sb3-the-live-pass-on-claude-partly-read) |

| (this change) | Agent design follows the ask (wave ag1, AG02): the agent runs on the host's builder model or the one Spotlight's picker names, never an architect upgrade (the card's Model is a select of the models the host offers), the design reads the whole ask (an address like `oncall@landflow.test` stays whole, later sentences kept, the nodes it names by display name added or listed under **Not added** with why), names are short nouns ("Alert triager", "Sales data analyst", "Grafana alerts agent", "Utility helper"), the steps name only the agent's own tools with a Tools line, a write is asked per app (reads such as SQLite Select never ask first), a job that reads tokens is not secret-masked on input, and the scenario runner runs on a cloud model without Ollama (`Start.plugins`, `sb-ag02.ts`) | [assistant.md](assistant.md#agents-and-teams) |

| (this change) | Spotlight changes an agent: a follow-up under a pending agent card revises the card in place instead of becoming a plan; under a created agent's card, or on an agent's page (the shell Spotlight's agent mode, chip "Changing <name>"), a change is a card of what would change with Apply to its draft (the draft only, then the smoke test; Publish after a pass, through the eval gate); a question is answered, never applied; the design route takes `base` and `change` and answers `changes`; tests `spotlight-agent-revise.test.ts`, scenarios `ag04-*` | [assistant.md](assistant.md#agents-and-teams) |
| (this change) | The agent smoke test says what really happened: not tested while a tool's required credential is unset, failed when a tool errors or returns nothing, and a denied write is named instead of failing it; publishing shows the eval gate's cases and a held verdict stays on the row, the card and the agent page; decided approval cards lose their buttons, and two calls waiting get Approve all | [Agents](agents.md#eval-gate), [Agents and teams](assistant.md#agents-and-teams) |
| (this change) | The SQLite credential has Create If Missing (a missing file is made when on, and the error names the option when off), an agent's node-tool dialog lists only the credentials its settings use (a pick leaves only when the person's change hides its slot), and a Send Email TLS error against a server that answers in plain text says to turn SSL/TLS off (Mailpit); tests `sqlite_create_test.go`, `agent-tool-credentials.test.ts`, `email-hint.test.ts`; the databases manifest fixtures re-recorded | [integrations.md](features/integrations.md) |

| (this change) | Wave ag1 carryover: a Spotlight agent card designed without the Agent architect (its call failed or it proposed nothing) says so: the reply reads "Made “X” from your words only …" and the card's Left row starts with "Designed without the architect: <why>" (the failed call's first line, at most 160 characters), from a `design` need `designAgent` adds to its fallback; sb3's live pass saw a fallback card that did not say so. Test `agent-design.test.ts`. | The reason rides in `needs`, so the design route's answer and the ag1 `agent-card.ts` contract stay as they are. |
| (this change) | The roadmap's wave ag1 section ends with what is left after it and why: the other Opus defaults (a person decides whether `LANDFLOW_BUILDER_MODEL` becomes every Anthropic node's default), a per-node tool input schema, the scorecard keeping replies, revising teams, and three questions owed to a person (a denied write as a pass, the agent page's Publish anyway, Acknowledge Alert by default). | Deferred rather than changed: each is a product decision or a change outside the wave's findings. |
| (this change) | Wave ag1 join fixes, found by the join tests: the smoke test's reason names a tool as a person reads it (the architect's `get_alerts` is "Get Alerts": *needs a credential: Get Alerts*, *Send Email needs approval: not sent*; `tools` keeps the config's names), via `agent-changes.ts`'s `shown`; a revise writes the steps' Tools line in the design's own form (`name (what it is for)`), and a Changes row no longer counts that line as "Instructions rewritten" (adding Slugify reads only *Adds the tool Slugify*); "also" with any verb ("also slugify titles in upper case") is a change under an agent card. | AG02 and AG04 each wrote the Tools line, in two forms; AG05's words used the config's snake_case names, which only the stand-ins of its own tests had as display names. |
| (this change) | Wave ag1 join tests (`apps/web/test/agents-ag1-joins.test.ts`): triage ask → card → node tool `item` → honest smoke (Send Email denied, nothing reaches a fake SMTP) → a held publish; Grafana's declared credential → card need → untested → the person's pick → passed or "returned nothing" against a fake Alertmanager; a revise in place, a question answered without a design, a created agent changed through its draft only; the chat's typed message reaches the starter's agent while Execute uses the pin and Unpin is saved; SQLite reads stay Select without approval; the ag1 contract copies (sb3's joins now point at the ag1 contracts that replaced two of theirs). | Real routes, plugins and stores; only the models, the browser client, SMTP and Alertmanager are fakes on loopback. |
| (this change) | Wave ag1 integration: a revise keeps AG02's promise. A tool the change names ("add Slugify as a tool") is added through the design's least-privilege rules even when the model's proposal leaves it out, and one that cannot be (no plugin online has it, several nodes match, a rule dropped it) is the answer's `notAdded`, the card's Not added row and the reply (*No change to the card above. Not added: Jira (no plugin online has it).*); *No change: … already has what you asked* only when nothing changed and nothing named was left out. A tool the change removes or refuses, or a new name's words, is not read as one, and a removed tool is never proposed back (`changeTools` in `architect.ts`, `toolsOf` exported from `agent-ask.ts`, `reviseReply` in the editor's `agent-ask.ts`; the agent page's change card shows Not added too). Tests: a join in `agents-ag1-joins.test.ts` §3 and the design route in `spotlight-agent-revise.test.ts` with a proposal that omits the named tool. |
| (this change) | AG02's two review blockers fixed. The instructions check (`nameOnlyOwnTools`) no longer reads a field as a foreign tool: a two-word `verb_noun` that ends like a field (`run_id`, `post_id`, `list_id`, `update_time`, now also `email_address`, `*_body`, `*_subject`, `*_text`) or follows a determiner (the, each, its, their, a, an, every, or `'s`) stays; a three-word one (`get_alert_status`) or one right after call, invoke, run or use is still a tool, so "Read the run_id and the post_id from the input." and "Sort by update_time, newest first." keep their steps. The ask's tools: "email a short summary to oncall@landflow.test" names the catalog's Send Email (a plugin node called Send … Email or Mail; the address is never changed), and "normalise" names Normalize Alert (-ise and -ize are one word). | `docs/assistant.md` (Agents from Spotlight); tests in `apps/web/test/agent-design-ag1.test.ts` (field names kept, the typed triage ask and a Jira variant with Send Email left out by the architect). |
| (this change) | A revise keeps the steps that call the agent's own tools. The live pass found "add Extract IOCs as a tool" under a triage card cut the *Call normalize_alert* and *Call send_email* steps (and left fragments such as "4. a short summary."): the instructions check (`nameOnlyOwnTools`) in `draftFrom` knew only the tools the change's proposal kept, and least privilege leaves out the tools a change does not name. The card's own tools (`keep`) now count as the agent's. | `docs/assistant.md` (Agents from Spotlight); a test in `apps/web/test/agent-design-ag1.test.ts` (it fails without the fix). |
| (this change) | Wave ag1 review fixes (second round). The instructions check reads a token as a tool before any field check when the next word is tool, node, action or function, or call/invoke/run (use, unless it ends like a field) precedes it with at most an article ("Call the get_alerts tool", "Send it via the slack_post tool"); a two-word token ending in to/by/for/from/of is a field (`send_to`). A revise counts the card's tools as its own only when it is a revise (`revise` on `draftFrom`/`runArchitect`), and `reviseDraft` runs the check again on the final tools: "remove the email tool" takes its step with it and says so; in the architect chat a dropped tool takes its step too; a base tool kept by a revise is no longer listed as "never asked for". One exported `MAIL_TO` (`forward` too) for `toolsOf` and the least-privilege rule; a mail node is taken only when exactly one matches, else Not added says *Send email: several nodes match (A, B), pick one*; analyse/analyze are one word; the triage scenario's script leaves out `messaging.emailSend`. The live pass's leftovers are in the roadmap ("Left after ag1") and limitations. | `docs/assistant.md` (Agents from Spotlight, Changing an agent); `docs/roadmap.md`; `docs/limitations.md`; tests in `apps/web/test/agent-design-ag1.test.ts` (they fail without the fix) |
| (this change) | Wave ag1 review fix: the article form of "use the X" / "run the X" reads a tool only for a tool-shaped name, so "Use the source_ip …", "Use the error_message …" and "run the post_id check" keep their steps | Opus re-review of 00e855c5 found field names after "use the" read as missing tools and their steps deleted; its verified patch, with a test that fails without it |

| (this change) | Wave ag2, AG10: Run an Agent has Tool Approvals (Refuse by default, or Ask in the editor's chat): in the editor's Chat a tool that needs approval waits for Approve and resumes the same agent session; every other mode refuses as before. The starter flow lays it out as Ask. Grafana's Acknowledge Alert posts to `/api/v2/silences` (Editor-role and URL messages) and Get Alert Details reads the alert by name through the list filter | `docs/agents.md` (From workflows); `docs/features/integrations.md`; `docs/assistant.md`; tests `apps/web/test/run-agent-chat-approvals.test.ts`, `plugins/grafana/test/silences.test.ts` |
| (this change) | Wave ag2, AG08. A design fills a node tool's destination only from the request (Send Email's To is the ask's address, its Subject and Text are `$fromAI`, From stays empty and asked for; HTTP Request's preset URL is left empty and asked for), a property the tool's schema lets the model fill per call (Acknowledge Alert's Alert ID) is no longer a "fill in" need, the eval cases are real inputs (a case that describes its input, is a `<placeholder>` or is under 12 characters is dropped, and the follow-up call asks for real ones), and a node tool takes only the arguments its schema offered (a tool with `$fromAI` placeholders or a `toolInputSchema` no longer takes a fixed To from the model). Docs only. |
| (this change) | Wave ag5, AG24: the eval gate compares a draft with the published version only on the cases both ran (two or more, by case id) and judges it against 3.0 alone when a change replaced them, saying which (`basis`, `shared`); a held verdict carries its score, basis, reason and lowest cases in the agent publish 409, the workflow publish's held answer and the publish card, on every attempt; revised instructions are cut outside brackets and every rewritten line must read as a sentence; a data agent's replacement cases never state what its tool holds, never re-add a removed case and name only documents its knowledge base holds (asked again once when fewer than two are left); the design route takes `keep` and never removes a tool the session added; `fix-autonomy.ts` copied from the ag5 contract | [agents.md](agents.md#eval-gate) |
| (this change) | Wave ag5, AG24 review fixes: a new design from a folder not indexed yet has its cases checked against the folder's file names; a kept case repeated is dropped with its own reason; an expected answer is checked only for runbook, playbook and policy nouns; replacement cases are capped at three; the same-cases reason and basis both say 3.0; the threshold basis carries no baseline; a failed instruction line never copies a neighbour; the *Kept* line only when the tool was put back. The live check ran on the production host with Claude (2026-11-18): the card added the SQLite tool with no removal and no figure in a case, the gate held the draft at 1.0 / 5 “on its new cases, below the 3.0” and the second attempt read the same score; found live: the revised instructions still carried a half sentence of the change text, and the SQLite tool came out *asks first*, so the gate's runs were denied. |

| (this change) | AG25 review fixes: a Run an Agent step that replaces an AI Agent now carries that AI Agent's system message, Chat Model and tool nodes to the Agent architect (`FlowDraft.newAgents`); "agent" as a person's role ("when a support agent closes…") or a `User-Agent` header no longer turns an AI Agent into a saved agent; docs say Undo takes two ⌘Z | [docs/assistant.md](assistant.md#agents-and-teams) |
| (this change) | AG25 review fixes 2: the design request cuts a long step message to 500 characters and instructions to 4000, so the agent card is still made | [docs/assistant.md](assistant.md#agents-and-teams) |
| (this change) | Wave ag5, AG22: the autonomous fix session. With the switch on, a Test and fix session (the button, a typed *made up, fix it*, the Fix it bubble, a resumed session) acts alone on any workflow, saved and published too, as the person decided on 2026-11-18 (*"We can let Spotlight approve automatically when he's fixing. It doesn't have to wait for permission in autonomous mode."*): it applies its flow and agent fixes (the agent change card shown applied, its draft saved and smoke-tested), runs to destinations the flow already sent to or the ask names with no *Run it now?*, sends `approvals: 'spotlight'` with its test runs, picks the one fitting credential, keeps its own fixes (`SessionFix[]`, `keep` on every design, a proposal that undoes one is dropped, the same fix twice stops it, a reply with an agent fix and flow proposals takes the agent fix), has the review read the agent step's reply, treats a held eval gate as a finding (the score, then one more fix from the lowest cases), and publishes each changed agent and then the workflow, ending with *Published: workflow v4 · Agent v5*. Asks first is unchanged; its header now reads *Needs you: apply the fix?* while an agent change card waits for Apply. New contract `lib/shared/fix-autonomy.ts`; the first-run note lists what Autonomous does and never does. Tests that asserted sb1's D1 for fix sessions were rewritten (a switch-on twin and a switch-off twin). Review fixes: the held gate reads AG24's real 409 (`details.gate.lowCases` with `reasoning`), Asks first keeps its design fix and handles an undo, and the two limitation lines this closes are struck through. | [assistant.md](assistant.md#autonomous-mode), [limitations.md](limitations.md) |
| (this change) | Wave ag5 integration: AG22's fix session meets the older suites. `spotlight-build-summary`'s mock of the test session gains `agentFixHooks` (the credential picker registers its hook on load); `agent-smoke-test`'s tests that read the asks-first end of a pass (one yes per scheme, the review's Publish action, the Needs-you publish row) run with the switch off, as the decision of 2026-11-18 makes the switch alone decide a fix session; a switch-on twin reads the session publishing the flow itself, and the autonomous *Publish … first* test wires the editor's save and reads the workflow published. The fix session's comment no longer names the flag the ag5 guard greps for. | [assistant.md](assistant.md#autonomous-mode) |
| (this change) | Wave ag5 carryover (live G): a session's run of a published Webhook flow with no pinned data stopped at *The trigger waits for a test event*, so the autonomous fix of a production flow never started. On the live rerun G went from P0's 400 to `publishedLine` with no click in 167 s | [docs/assistant.md](assistant.md#test-and-fix) |
| (this change) | Wave ag5 carryover (join 1): AG24's design route puts back a tool the session added (`keep`) and says so in `dropped`, so AG22's `agentUndoneBy` never saw the tool missing and a held-gate round whose design removed it saved and re-published a change that changed nothing. `showAgentFix` now reads the route's *Kept “X”: this session added it* line for a tool this session added as the undo: the design is dropped with `keptOwnFixLine` and the session stops (`test/fix-path-ag5-joins.test.ts`, join 1's held branch, fails without it) | [docs/assistant.md](assistant.md#autonomous-mode) |
| (this change) | Wave ag5 carryover (live G2): a typed *Send the email from landflow@landflow.test.* on a saved agent came back *No change*: the chat wrote the change as *Set send_email's fixed fromEmail parameter to …*, which ag3's sender rule did not read, so From Email stayed empty and every test run's mail failed (*From Email is required*). The rule now reads `fromEmail`/`From Email` followed by *parameter* or *field* too; two different senders still set nothing (`test/agent-revise-sender-ag3.test.ts`) | [docs/agents.md](agents.md) |
| (this change) | Wave ag5 joins (`apps/web/test/fix-path-ag5-joins.test.ts`): the four packages on their real sides. Every browser `api`/`streamEvents` call goes to the `route.ts` its path names; the editor and assistant stores are the real ones, with the editor's run (`execute`) and save done through the run and workflow routes. Join 1: a published Webhook flow whose agent invents figures, after a production 400: the session tests with the trigger's last call, fixes the body (`report_text`) with no click, reads the agent's reply in the review, connects the sales read (the one fitting credential picked), publishes the agent on its new cases (`basis: 'threshold'`) and the workflow (`publishedLine`), and production then posts the figures (*workflow vN · Short writer v2*); its held branch says *2.0 / 5 … (judged on its new cases)*, drops a design that would take the added tool back (`keptOwnFixLine`) and never sends `skipEval` or `as-published`. Join 2: Spotlight approves Send Email to the given address (one mail, `approvedByLine`, the session's `by`), the unattended line after the publish, a production run still refuses; an address nobody gave waits; Asks first sends no approvals; a viewer and a test-update run are refused the field. Join 3: an inline AI Agent becomes a Run an Agent step whose agent is designed, created and published on a brand-new draft, and the session's review reads its reply. Join 4: a held publish carries its score and basis through `publishChange`, two `publish-first` workflow publishes, the session's line and the publish card. Join 5: `fix-autonomy.ts` byte for byte with `contracts/ag5` (`LANDFLOW_BACKBONE_CONTRACTS`). Join 6: no made-up secret in any answer, run or session | [docs/development.md](development.md) |
